- [x] AD-238 Wire `/admin/support/conversations` ("Product Conversations")
      to real data. It was a fully mock duplicate of the already-DB-backed
      `/admin/questions` (ProductQuestion) screen — same underlying
      customer-asks-about-a-product data, static rows, and a "View" button
      that only toasted. Now loads `loadAdminQuestionList()` and its View
      button links to the existing real `/admin/questions/[id]` answer
      page instead of duplicating a second Q&A system. Removed the now-dead
      `MOCK_PRODUCT_CONVERSATIONS` / `ProductConversation` mock export.
- [x] AD-239 Admin → Contacts replies now send real email. `/admin/contacts`
      itself was already fully DB-wired (AD-224); the gap was that a staff
      "Reply" only ever saved to `Complaint.reply` — nothing was delivered
      to the customer. Added the app's first outbound-mail capability:
      `nodemailer` dependency + lib/mail/send.ts (`sendMail`/`sendMailSafe`),
      reading the saved `SmtpConfiguration` (host/port/username/encryption/
      from) plus the `SMTP_PASSWORD` env var (never stored in DB, per the
      existing convention from GATEWAY_SECRETS_KEY). `updateAdminContact`
      emails the customer when the reply text is new/changed and surfaces
      a non-fatal "Saved, but the email did not send: …" warning if SMTP
      isn't configured — the DB save still succeeds either way. Requires
      the operator to set `SMTP_PASSWORD` and real SMTP settings in
      Admin → Setup → SMTP to actually deliver; untested against a live
      mail server in this environment.
- [x] AD-240 Admin → OTP "Send test OTP" now sends a real SMS. The gateway
      config save was already DB-wired; the test-send button always said
      "SMS delivery is deferred". Added lib/sms/send.ts (`sendSms`) with
      per-provider HTTP calls (ssl-wireless, mim-sms, twilio, messagebird;
      local-mock intentionally never sends) reading SMS_API_KEY/
      SMS_API_SECRET (never stored in DB — field mapping differs per
      provider, documented in the file). New sendTestOtpAction in
      features/admin/otp/otp-actions.ts (permission-gated via otp.manage).
      Twilio/MessageBird calls follow their stable public REST APIs
      (high confidence); SSL Wireless/Mim SMS request shapes are
      best-effort from public docs and not verified against a live
      account — operator should confirm against current provider docs.
      Scope explicitly excludes the real login/registration OTP
      generate/verify flow (auth-security-critical, deferred per
      operator decision) — the toggles still only store intent.
- [x] AD-241 Wire Nagad into real checkout (was admin-config-only). bKash
      and SSLCommerz were already fully real (hosted session, callback
      verification, refunds) — Nagad had encrypted admin credentials but
      no adapter and wasn't even selectable at checkout. Added:
      lib/payments/nagad-crypto.ts (RSA encrypt/sign/decrypt via Node's
      built-in crypto, mirroring Nagad's PHP openssl_* sample pattern —
      no new dependency), lib/payments/nagad.ts (initialize → complete →
      verify, matching the bKash/SSLCommerz adapter shape), Nagad added to
      PaymentProviderId/PaymentMethodId/HostedGatewayId,
      loadDecryptedNagadFromDb in gateway-settings.ts, Nagad domains added
      to the payment-redirect allowlist, and a new
      app/api/payments/nagad/callback/route.ts. Checkout UI
      (checkout-view.tsx) now shows Nagad as a selectable method gated on
      real config presence, same as the other two. `npm run test:payments`
      (47 checks) passes. Nagad's exact field names/endpoints/headers are
      best-effort from public integration guides and untested against a
      live Nagad sandbox — verify before going live (docs/PAYMENT_SECURITY.md
      updated with this caveat). Nagad refunds are intentionally NOT
      automated (no confirmed public refund API) — payoutRefund already
      falls through to "cannot be refunded here" for nagad, unchanged.
- [x] AD-242 Verified bKash/SSLCommerz against their official docs
      (developer.bka.sh, developer.sslcommerz.com) and fixed real bugs
      found:
      - bKash Create Payment was posting to `/tokenized/checkout/create`
        (the mode-0001 agreement/recurring path, needs an agreementID we
        never send) instead of `/tokenized/checkout/payment/create`
        (mode-0011 one-time, correct for our flow). This alone likely
        broke bKash checkout against any real (non-lenient-sandbox)
        account.
      - bKash Execute Payment was posting `{paymentID}` in the JSON body
        to `/tokenized/checkout/execute`; the real API takes paymentID as
        a URL path segment: `/tokenized/checkout/execute/{paymentID}`,
        no body.
      - bKash Refund was on the wrong path/API version
        (`/tokenized/checkout/payment/refund`) with wrong field casing
        (`paymentID`/`trxID`/`amount`, reading `refundTrxID`). Real API:
        `bkashRefundUrl()` → a "v2" path
        (`/v2/tokenized-checkout/refund/payment/transaction`) with
        `paymentId`/`trxId`/`refundAmount`, reading `refundTrxId` — the
        casing mismatch alone meant refunds could never find a ref even
        if the old endpoint had accepted the call.
      - bKash's query-payment fallback (secondary check when Execute
        doesn't report "Completed") switched from an unconfirmed
        `/tokenized/checkout/payment/status` POST to the documented GET
        `/tokenized/checkout/payment/query/{paymentID}` — still
        best-effort since that endpoint is filed under a different
        product family in bKash's own docs.
      - SSLCommerz: session-create and IPN-validate matched the docs
        exactly (no changes). Refund was sending `refe_id`; the real
        field name is `refund_trans_id` — fixed.
      Added `gatewayGet(url, headers?)` support (bKash query needs
      Authorization/X-App-Key on a GET) and `bkashRefundUrl()` in
      lib/payments/config.ts (v2 host, separate from the v1.2.0-beta
      base). `npm run test:payments` (47 checks) and `npm run build`
      both pass. None of this was testable against live bKash/SSLCommerz
      sandboxes here — the fixes are sourced directly from the official
      API references, but the operator should still run a real sandbox
      transaction (create → execute → refund) before trusting this live.
- [x] AD-243 Wire `/admin/payments/offline` and `/admin/payments/emi` to
      real DB-backed settings, plus the storefront wiring that makes them
      workable. New Prisma models `OfflinePaymentSettings` and
      `EmiSettings` (singleton rows, migration
      20260908122742_offline_emi_payment_settings), lib/payments/
      offline-config.ts + emi-config.ts (server), lib/payments/emi-shared.ts
      (client-safe constants/types — EMI_TENURE_OPTIONS must not be
      imported by value into a client component, same anti-pattern as
      AD-235/238), features/admin/payments/offline-emi-actions.ts
      (permission-gated via payment_methods.manage / emi.manage). Checkout
      (`/checkout`) now shows the real COD label/instructions and hides it
      when disabled, enforced server-side too in
      lib/payments/service.ts::preparePaymentStart (client-side disabling
      alone is never trusted for money-moving state). The PDP EMI teaser
      (features/product/product-summary.tsx) now only renders when EMI is
      enabled and the order total meets the configured minimum, shows the
      real tenure list/partner name/interest note, and the fake "View
      plans" toast button is gone. Also fixed a stale "(mock)" payment
      note on the PDP unrelated to this in the same paragraph.
      Mid-fix, found and fixed the exact AD-235-shaped bug again:
      admin-emi-settings.tsx value-imported EMI_TENURE_OPTIONS from the
      Prisma-touching emi-config.ts, breaking the whole dev server (not
      just this page) — split into emi-shared.ts. Also found `prisma
      migrate dev` had NOT actually regenerated the Prisma client despite
      the migration applying — `emiSettings`/`offlinePaymentSettings`
      were missing from the generated client until an explicit `npx
      prisma generate`. `npm run test:payments` (47 checks) passes;
      `tsc --noEmit` clean; all touched routes manually verified 200 on a
      restarted dev server. (`npm run build` was NOT used for final
      verification here — running it while the user's own dev server was

      unrelated to the actual code; see PROJECT_STATE.md pending notes.)

      Operator chose consolidation. Removed Countries/States/Cities/

- [x] AD-253 Real "Send to Pathao" / "Send to Steadfast" courier booking
      from Admin → Orders (WordPress/WooCommerce-style button per row —
      operator's explicit request). New `CourierSetting` model (migration
      20260908174522_courier_settings, mirrors PaymentGatewaySetting
      exactly), lib/shipping/courier-secret-crypto.ts (its own
      COURIER_SECRETS_KEY, separate from GATEWAY_SECRETS_KEY/
      STORAGE_SECRETS_KEY per the established per-domain-key convention),
      lib/shipping/courier-settings.ts (admin CRUD), lib/shipping/
      couriers/steadfast.ts + pathao.ts (the real HTTP clients),
      lib/orders/send-to-courier.ts (orchestration: always re-fetches the
      order server-side rather than trusting client PII, and refuses to
      send an order that already has a carrierId — a real external
      booking can't be un-sent, so double-booking is blocked outright).
      Reused the Order model's existing but previously-dead `carrierId`
      column (only `trackingCode` was ever written, via manual staff
      entry) — now both get set for real on a successful send, so the
      customer-facing /track page (which already reads trackingCode)
      picks it up with no changes needed there.

      Confidence differs sharply between the two couriers — verified with
      web search + fetches today, not from memory alone:
      - **Steadfast**: HIGH confidence. Base URL
        (portal.packzy.com/api/v1), `Api-Key`/`Secret-Key` headers, and
        create_order field names (invoice, recipient_name/phone/address,
        cod_amount) were independently corroborated across Steadfast's
        own WordPress plugin listing and multiple current integration
        guides.
      - **Pathao**: LOWER confidence. Official docs sit behind a
        merchant-login portal that couldn't be reached. Field names for
        order creation are corroborated (store_id, recipient_name/phone/
        address/city/zone/area, delivery_type, item_type,
        amount_to_collect), but the exact base URLs
        (api-hermes.pathao.com / courier-api-sandbox.pathao.com), the
        "aladdin" endpoint path prefix, and the delivery_type/item_type
        numeric codes (48/2) are best-effort general knowledge, NOT
        independently reconfirmed today — flagged in code comments.
        Operator should verify these against their own Pathao merchant
        dashboard before a real send.

      Pathao also structurally needs a recipient city/zone/area (from
      Pathao's own lookup lists) that our orders don't store — asked the
      operator how to handle this rather than guessing; they chose a
      live picker. "Send to Pathao" opens
      features/admin/orders/send-to-pathao-modal.tsx, which fetches
      Pathao's real city → zone → area lists live (cascading dropdowns)
      before submitting. Steadfast needs no such picker — free-text
      address is all it takes.

      Also wired the credentials UI: the Pathao/Steadfast cards on

      credentials screen. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run test:payments`

      Operator explicitly acknowledged this needs real-account testing
      before trusting it live ("amra pore test korbo eta") — matches the
      Nagad precedent (AD-241) for how this codebase handles best-effort
      third-party courier/payment integrations.
- [x] AD-254 District/Upazila checkout selection + weight-based Inside
      Dhaka / Outside Dhaka shipping rates (operator's explicit request:
      "shipping 2ta jinish thakbe, district, upozila... zilla wisem upzila
      thakbe shipping setting ta vabe manage korbe eta frontend e thakbe,
      checkout e"). Two AskUserQuestion rounds resolved: (1) pricing stays
      exactly the existing 2-zone Inside/Outside Dhaka model — no per-
      district or per-upazila rates — and IS weight-based, operator asked
      me to design the mechanism; (2) seed all 64 real districts now
      (rather than Dhaka-only or empty); (3) confirmed formula: base rate
      for first N grams + flat extra per additional kg, per zone.

      Deliberately built as a UI/data layer that resolves DOWN into the
      EXISTING ShippingZone/ShippingArea/Cart.shippingAreaId pipeline
      rather than a parallel geography or pricing system — zero changes
      to Cart/Order schema or checkout-submission logic beyond passing one
      new totalWeightGrams number through. Migration
      20260908181707_district_upazila_weight_shipping: ShippingZone gained
      baseWeightGrams/baseRateAmount/extraRatePerKgAmount; new District
      (name, zoneId, position) and Upazila (districtId, name,
      shippingAreaId nullable FK to ShippingArea) models; Product gained
      weightGrams (default 500g — see caveat below). lib/shipping/
      bd-districts-seed.ts holds all 64 districts + upazilas (best-effort
      general knowledge, NOT verified against an official source — flagged
      for the operator to spot-check); scripts/shipping/seed-districts.ts
      is the idempotent upsert seed (ran: 64 districts, 486 upazilas,
      one ShippingArea per upazila named "{upazila} — {district}").

      Pricing: lib/cart/shipping.ts's calculateZoneShippingAmount(zone,
      totalWeightGrams) = baseRateAmount + ceil(max(0, totalWeightGrams -
      baseWeightGrams) / 1000) * extraRatePerKgAmount. resolveShippingRate
      gained a totalWeightGrams param and now prices non-pickup methods
      through this instead of the old flat method.baseRate. New Admin →
      Shipping → Shipping Rates page (/admin/shipping/rates, gated on
      shipping_config.manage to save / shipping_config.view to view) lets
      the operator set each zone's base weight/rate/extra-per-kg with a
      live preview. Checkout (features/checkout/checkout-view.tsx) gained
      real District → Upazila cascading selects (real 64/486 data, not
      mock); selecting an upazila drives the existing shipping-method
      price/eligibility logic unchanged. Cart weight is fetched via a
      dedicated lib/shipping/product-weights.ts + features/checkout/
      weight-actions.ts server action (kept separate from the shared
      ProductSummary type on purpose — client-preview-only concern).

      **Bug found and fixed during verification (self-caught, not
      operator-reported)**: lib/shipping/districts.ts's
      listPublicUpazilas() originally returned the raw ShippingArea.id
      (cuid) as each upazila's shippingAreaId. Every other consumer of
      "public" shipping area ids (lib/shipping/locations.ts's
      listPublicShippingAreas, and therefore all of cart/checkout/order
      creation) uses a different composite scheme instead —
      `${zoneCode}::${areaName}` via publicAreaId() — specifically so
      cart state survives re-seeds. The mismatch meant every District/
      Upazila selection at checkout would have silently failed to resolve
      a shipping rate (resolveShippingRate returning { ok: false } for
      every real combination, 100% of the time) despite all other wiring
      being correct. Fixed by having listPublicUpazilas() join through
      Upazila.shippingArea and build the same publicAreaId(zone.code,
      area.name) string. Verified with a temporary script
      (scripts/_tmp-verify-shipping.ts, deleted after use) that drove
      calculateZoneShippingAmount + resolveShippingRate end-to-end with
      real seeded Dhaka/Cumilla upazilas and temporary zone rates
      (60/20 and 120/30) — confirmed correct base + per-kg-extra math
      before reverting those zone rates back to the unconfigured default
      (0/0) so no invented test prices are left live for the operator.

      Also carried forward the AD-253 mid-task fix: Order.carrierId is a
      real FK to ShippingCarrier (not free text) — discovered while
      re-reading the Order model for this feature; lib/orders/
      send-to-courier.ts now resolves/upserts a real ShippingCarrier row
      via resolveCarrierId() before writing it, and lib/admin/
      load-orders.ts now joins through the carrier relation instead of
      selecting a raw scalar.

      `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run test:payments` 47/47 (both after the
      composite-id fix), dev server restarted, home/checkout routes
      verified live (checkout correctly shows empty-cart state for an
      unauthenticated request — expected, not a bug; full District→
      Upazila form only renders for a populated authenticated cart so
      could not be curl-tested end-to-end).

      - The seeded upazila list (64 districts / 486 upazilas) is
        best-effort and should be spot-checked/corrected via future admin
        tooling if the operator finds anything missing or misspelled.
      - Incidentally observed, NOT touched: all 3 ShippingMethod rows
        currently have isActive=false (cause/timing unclear, occurred

        least one under Admin → Shipping → Select Shipping Method.
- [x] AD-255 Made the Staff section (Admin → Staff) real, per the
      operator's request to "make dynamic stuff [staff] section feature."
      Roles/permissions/audit log were already fully DB-backed
      (lib/auth/staff-roles.ts, role-actions.ts, listAuditLogs) — the real
      gap, found by reading the code first per the established workflow,
      was that the actual "Add staff" / "Edit staff" form
      (features/admin/staff/admin-staff-form.tsx, used by both
      /admin/staff/new and every /admin/staff/[id] edit page) never wrote
      to the database at all: `handleSubmit` only called `notifySuccess`
      with a "(mock)" toast and pushed back to the list. On top of that,
      there was no way whatsoever to create a staff account except direct
      DB access — `lib/auth/staff-auth.ts` says outright "There is no
      self-serve staff registration" — so this admin form was the only
      intended path and it was completely fake.

      Also found and fixed a lossy display bug on the read side: the
      staff list/detail already read from real `Staff`/`Role` rows, but
      `toAdminStaff()` in lib/admin/load-settings.ts ran every real role
      key through a crude `toStaffRole()` substring-matcher that force-fit
      it into one of only 3 hardcoded buckets (admin/manager/support) —
      any custom role created via the real (working) Roles UI would have
      displayed as the wrong bucket everywhere. New lib/admin/
      staff-types.ts defines the real `AdminStaffMember` type with
      `role: { id, key, name } | null` sourced directly from the DB
      relation; deleted `toStaffRole()` entirely.

      Built the real save path: new lib/admin/save-staff.ts (`saveStaff`)
      — validates via the existing shared lib/account/validation.ts
      helpers (same email/name/phone/password rules as customer
      accounts, not invented fresh), hashes passwords with the existing
      lib/auth/password.ts Argon2id helper (same one staff login already
      verifies against), looks up the real Role by key, catches the
      Prisma P2002 unique-email conflict with a friendly message, and
      blocks a staff member from setting their own account to a non-
      "active" status (self-lockout guard). New features/admin/staff/
      staff-actions.ts (`saveStaffAction`) is the "use server" wrapper —
      same-origin check, gated on `staff.add` for create / `staff.edit`
      for update (both real permissions already in the seeded catalogue,
      granted to the Admin system role, denied to Manager — confirmed via
      prisma/seed.ts before wiring, not guessed), writes new
      AUDIT_ACTIONS.STAFF_CREATE/STAFF_UPDATE entries (passwords are
      never included — writeAuditLog's sanitizeMetadata already strips
      password-shaped keys as a second layer on top of never passing one
      in).

      Rewrote admin-staff-form.tsx to actually submit via
      `saveStaffAction` (useTransition, matching admin-role-form.tsx's
      established real-submit shape) instead of a local-state toast; role
      dropdown now lists real roles from `listStaffRoles()` instead of
      the fake `MOCK_STAFF_ROLES`; added a real Status field (Active/
      Invited/Disabled — the schema already had this, just never exposed)
      so an operator can actually disable a staff account, disabled when
      editing your own account with an inline explanation. Both
      /admin/staff/new and /admin/staff/[id] pages now check
      `staff.add`/`staff.edit` server-side (mirroring the existing
      roles/new pattern) and pass real roles + self-flag through.

      Deleted two now-fully-orphaned files as part of this cleanup (zero
      remaining references, confirmed by grep before deleting):
      features/admin/staff/admin-permissions-matrix.tsx (an old

      Verified with a temporary script (scripts/_tmp-verify-staff.ts,

- [x] AD-256 Made Admin → Customers real, per the operator's "make it
      dynamic, make customer section dynamic" (URL: /admin/customers).
      The list and detail READ side were already fully DB-backed
      (lib/admin/load-customers.ts, reading the real `User` table) — the
      gap was entirely on the write side: "Add customer" only toasted
      "(mock)" and never touched the database; the customer-detail page's
      Status/Notes "Save changes" button was the same; every per-row menu
      action (Log in as this customer, Ban/Unban, Mark/Clear suspicious,
      Wallet recharge, Delete) and the list's bulk-action dropdown
      (Ban/Unban/Mark suspicious/Delete) were 100% fake `notifySuccess
      (mock)` calls with zero backing logic.

      Before building, surfaced 4 genuinely ambiguous/risky pieces to the
      operator via AskUserQuestion rather than deciding unilaterally
      (matching the AD-252/254 precedent for scope questions):
      1. **Wallet recharge** — no wallet-transaction ledger table exists,
         only a running `User.walletAmount` balance. Operator chose the
         simple option: a direct balance adjustment (audit-logged, no
         queryable history) over building a new ledger model.
      2. **Delete** — a real hard-delete would cascade through a
         customer's orders/reviews/tickets, which is wrong for an
         e-commerce record with real history. Operator chose to drop
         "Delete" entirely rather than keep a deceptively-labeled button
         that just bans — Ban/Unban already covers the real use case, so
         the redundant "Delete" entry was removed from both the row menu
         and the bulk-action dropdown (not relabeled/kept).
      3. **"Log in as this customer" (impersonation)** — operator chose
         to skip it this round rather than ship staff-impersonation
         without dedicated review; the button was removed rather than
         left fake. Flagged as a real, separate future task if wanted —
         it would need its own audited session mechanism, not a field
         update.
      4. **B2B accounts section** (/admin/customers/b2b) — found to be a
         fully disconnected mock: fake companies with fake ids/emails,
         not linked to any real `User`, plus a mock storefront-facing B2B
         signup dialog (features/b2b/b2b-auth-dialog.tsx,
         lib/b2b/mock-session.ts). The real schema already has a
         `B2BAccount` model tied to a real `User.id`, so making this real
         means designing an actual approve/reject workflow against real
         customers — a distinct subsystem, not a save-button wire-up.
         Operator chose to leave it for a separate task. **Zero changes
         made to any B2B file this round** — left exactly as found.

      What was actually built: new lib/admin/save-customer.ts —
      `createCustomer` (Argon2id password hash via the same
      lib/auth/password.ts helper Staff/customer login already use;
      no invite-token flow exists for customers so, like AD-255 Staff,
      the admin sets a real password directly), `updateCustomerProfile`
      (status + notes), `setCustomerBanned` / `bulkSetCustomerBanned`
      (maps to the real `BLOCKED`/`ACTIVE` UserStatus), `setCustomerSuspicious`
      / `bulkSetCustomerSuspicious` (`User.isSuspicious`), and
      `adjustCustomerWallet` (validates a non-zero integer amount,
      rejects a deduction that would take the balance below zero, inside
      a `$transaction` to avoid a lost-update race). Duplicate email/
      phone conflicts (P2002) return a friendly message instead of a raw
      Prisma throw, distinguishing which field conflicted from Prisma's
      `meta.target`. New features/admin/customers/customer-actions.ts
      wraps all of these as "use server" actions, each gated on the real
      seeded permission it actually maps to (`customer.add`,
      `customer.edit`, `customer.ban` — confirmed already in the
      catalogue, not invented) plus the standard same-origin check. New
      AUDIT_ACTIONS: CUSTOMER_CREATE/UPDATE/BAN/UNBAN/WALLET_ADJUST.

      Rewired admin-customer-form.tsx (real create + password field,
      useTransition), admin-customer-detail.tsx (real Save via
      `updateCustomerProfileAction`; also deleted a stale "Mock customer
      profile / arrives with Phase 11 authentication" banner that was
      long outdated — Phase 11 auth has been live all session),
      admin-customer-row-actions.tsx (Ban/Unban, Mark/Clear suspicious,
      and Wallet recharge — a plain `window.prompt()` for the amount,
      matching the "simple" scope decision rather than building a new
      dialog component — all wired for real; impersonation and Delete
      removed), and admin-customer-list.tsx's bulk-action select (same
      three real actions via new bulk server actions; "Delete" removed).
      /admin/customers/new and /admin/customers/[id] now check
      `customer.add`/`customer.edit` server-side (mirroring the AD-255
      Staff pattern) and the detail page hides the Save button entirely
      for a viewer without `customer.edit`.

      Verified against real Postgres with a temporary script (deleted
      after use): create stores a genuine Argon2id hash that verifies
      correctly; duplicate email is rejected cleanly; profile
      status/notes update persists; ban → BLOCKED and unban → ACTIVE
      both confirmed by re-reading the row; suspicious flag toggles
      correctly; wallet +500 then -200 nets to 300, and attempting to
      deduct 10,000 more is correctly rejected without touching the
      balance; bulk ban and bulk clear-suspicious on the same row both
      apply correctly. All test rows cleaned up after. `tsc --noEmit`
      clean, `npm run test:payments` 47/47, /admin/customers,
      /admin/customers/new, and /admin/customers/b2b all verified live
      (307 to login, no 404/500 — b2b route deliberately untouched and
      still mock, confirmed still serving).

      operator's explicit follow-up to AD-256 ("make it dynamic, its
      connect with frontend" on /admin/customers/b2b) — the piece
      deliberately deferred last round. Spawned a research agent first to
      map the full surface area before touching anything, since "connect
      with frontend" meant this was a real feature to design, not a
      wire-up: found the real `B2BAccount`/`B2BStatus` schema (tied 1:1 to
      `User`) already existed and was completely unused by any Prisma
      query anywhere, the admin side was a disconnected mock array (fake
      companies, fake ids, status casing that didn't even match the DB
      enum), and the storefront ran an entirely separate, parallel mock
      auth system (features/b2b/use-b2b-session.ts) — its own register/
      sign-in in `localStorage`, **plaintext password stored client-
      side**, fake file "uploads" (filename only), and a hardcoded flat
      12% wholesale discount (lib/b2b/wholesale-price.ts) completely
      ignoring the real per-account `discountPercent`/`tier` fields.

      The schema itself settles the architecture: since `B2BAccount.userId`
      is unique to a real `User`, B2B can only correctly be "an
      application layered on an existing real customer account," not a
      separate identity system — so the fix removes the whole parallel
      mock auth/session stack rather than persisting it. Confirmed this
      plan and two genuinely open scope questions with the operator via
      AskUserQuestion before building (same pattern as AD-254/256):
      1. **Document storage** — trade licence/NID were never really
         uploaded. Operator chose real file storage over leaving it
         fake. Built lib/b2b/document-storage.ts specifically for this:
         unlike the admin media library (public/uploads — meant to be
         publicly servable product images), these are personal KYC
         documents, so they're written to a NEW `private-uploads/b2b/`
         directory OUTSIDE `public/` (added to .gitignore) that Next.js
         never serves directly; retrieval only via a new authenticated
         route (below). Extension allowlist (jpg/png/webp/pdf), 8MB cap,
         and a strict filename-pattern check on read to block path
         traversal (verified in testing — see below).
      2. **Discount application scope** — operator chose PDP display
         only for now (not cart/checkout totals), keeping this out of
         the security-critical order-pricing path per the top-level
         CLAUDE.md instruction to treat payment-adjacent work carefully.

      New lib/b2b/applications.ts (storefront-facing): `getMyB2BAccount`,
      `applyForB2B` — validates fields, requires both documents, blocks a
      duplicate application while PENDING or ACTIVE but explicitly
      *allows* re-applying after SUSPENDED (resets status to PENDING,
      clears approvedAt, keeps the previous discount/tier as a sensible
      admin-review default rather than resetting to 0). New "use server"
      features/b2b/b2b-apply-actions.ts requires a real
      `getCustomerSession()` — no session, no application; same-origin
      checked. New features/b2b/b2b-apply-dialog.tsx (replacing the old
      B2BAuthDialog) is a real form using the existing Dialog primitive —
      company/contact/address text fields plus two real file inputs,
      submitted as FormData directly to the server action.

      New lib/admin/b2b-accounts.ts (admin-facing): `listAdminB2BAccounts`
      (tab-filtered Pending/Active/Suspended/All, search, paginated),
      `getAdminB2BAccountById` (joins the real customer's name/email/
      phone), `approveB2BAccount` (sets ACTIVE + `approvedAt`, discount/
      tier/notes), `updateB2BAccount` (adjust terms on an already-active
      account), `setB2BAccountSuspended` (+ reactivate). New
      features/admin/customers/b2b-actions.ts gates every mutation on the
      real, already-seeded `customer.b2b.manage` permission (view-level
      gating was already wired into admin-route-permissions.ts before
      this task). New authenticated route
      app/(admin)/admin/api/b2b-documents/route.ts streams a document
      back to staff — deliberately placed under `/admin/...` (not
      `/api/...`) because the staff session cookie is `Path=/admin` and
      would never reach a route outside it (documented explicitly in the
      existing order-alerts route's own comment, which this one copies).
      Rewrote features/admin/customers/admin-b2b-accounts.tsx as a real
      list + detail/approval UI (status tabs, document view links,
      tier/discount inputs, Approve/Suspend/Reactivate).

      PDP integration: app/(storefront)/product/[slug]/page.tsx now
      fetches the signed-in customer's real B2BAccount alongside its
      existing session-dependent data (own reviews/questions — this page
      was already dynamically rendered per-request via `cookies()`, so
      this added no new caching concern) and passes it through
      product-media-buy.tsx into product-summary.tsx, which now computes
      the wholesale price for real via a new pure lib/b2b/pricing.ts
      (`computeWholesalePrice`) instead of the old hardcoded 12%, and
      shows the correct state per real status: signed-out → link to
      login; signed-in with no account → "apply" button opening the real
      dialog; PENDING → "under review"; ACTIVE → real wholesale price +
      company name; SUSPENDED → "suspended — re-apply". Deleted the four
      fully-superseded mock files (features/b2b/b2b-auth-dialog.tsx,
      features/b2b/use-b2b-session.ts, lib/b2b/mock-session.ts,
      lib/b2b/wholesale-price.ts) and lib/admin/b2b-mock.ts after
      confirming zero remaining references.

      **Bug hit and fixed mid-task**: the exact recurring client/server
      Prisma-leak shape (found ~5 times already this session) — the new
      admin-b2b-accounts.tsx ("use client") value-imported `adminB2BHref`
      from lib/admin/b2b-accounts.ts, which also has `getPrisma` at its
      top, pulling `pg` (and therefore Node's `dns` module) into the
      browser bundle and 500-ing the *entire app*, not just this page
      (`npm run test:payments`' two HTTP-based checks caught it
      immediately — home page itself was returning 500). Fixed exactly
      per the established precedent: extracted the pure, client-safe
      pieces (`adminB2BHref`, `parseAdminB2BParams`, `AdminB2BTab`) into
      a new lib/admin/b2b-list-shared.ts, matching the existing
      lib/admin/staff-list-shared.ts pattern, and re-pointed the client
      component's import there while the server pages keep using
      lib/admin/b2b-accounts.ts directly. Confirmed fixed by re-running
      `npm run test:payments` (back to 47/47) and curling the homepage
      (200 again).

      Verified against real Postgres with a temporary script (deleted
      after use): the pure discount math (1000 @ 12% → 880); a real
      apply creates a PENDING row with two genuinely-stored, byte-correct
      documents; duplicate apply while PENDING/ACTIVE is blocked with the
      right message; admin approve sets ACTIVE + the chosen discount/
      tier and that exact state is what the customer-facing lookup
      returns; suspend sets SUSPENDED; re-applying after suspension
      resets to PENDING with the new documents; and `readB2BDocument`
      correctly refuses a path-traversal key (`../../../etc/passwd`) and
      returns null instead of reading outside its storage root. All test
      rows and document files cleaned up after. `tsc --noEmit` clean,
      `npm run test:payments` 47/47 (after the mid-task fix), PDP and
      admin B2B routes verified live (PDP 200 for a real product,
      correctly showing "For B2B — sign in for wholesale price" to an
      unauthenticated request; admin B2B list/detail routes 307 to
      login).
- [x] AD-258 Coupons + Marketing sidebar audit, then built the pieces the
      operator chose. Spawned a research agent to audit Coupons and the

      Findings: **Coupons was already fully real end-to-end** (creation,

      lib/marketing/coupons.ts, called from lib/orders/create-order.ts) —

      Also already real: Newsletters, Blog, Subscribers, Notifications →

      Fully mock, needing genuine new architecture (not a save-button
      wire-up): Dynamic Pop-ups, Custom Alerts, Custom Sale Alert, Email

      chose **Dynamic Pop-ups** and **Custom Visitors (real tracking)**
      this round; deferred Custom Alerts, Custom Sale Alert, Email
      Templates, and Bulk SMS. Also fixed two smaller fully-mock pages

      Schema (migration 20260908203324_marketing_notifications_popups_
      visitors, plus a follow-up 20260909023657_notification_type_name_
      unique applied via `prisma db execute` + `prisma migrate resolve
      --applied` since `migrate dev` refused to run non-interactively for
      a unique-constraint warning — table was empty, confirmed safe):
      `NotificationTypeSetting` (real catalogue, seeded with the same 11
      default types the mock UI had — Order Placed/Confirmed/Picked Up/
      etc. — via a new idempotent block in prisma/seed.ts; explicitly
      NOT yet wired to automatic order-lifecycle triggers, since no code
      anywhere creates a Notification keyed to one of these today — that
      remains a separate, larger task), `NotificationSettings` (singleton,
      same shape as RefundSettings/FilesystemSettings), `Popup` (real
      admin-authored pop-up, matches the existing form's exact fields:
      title/summary/image/buttonText/buttonColor/buttonTextTone/link,
      plus a new per-popup `delaySeconds` replacing the old fake *global*
      "duration" control), `ProductViewEvent` (one real page-view event
      keyed to an anonymous per-browser cookie id — never an IP or
      account id — indexed on `[productId, viewedAt]`, pruned
      opportunistically on write), `VisitorWidgetSettings` (singleton:
      enabled/windowMinutes/minToShow).

      **Notifications Types/Settings**: new lib/notifications/
      type-settings.ts + global-settings.ts, actions added to the
      existing features/admin/notifications/notification-actions.ts,
      gated on the real `notifications.manage` permission. Removed the
      fake "Image (36×36)" upload field from the type form and its table
      column entirely — it never stored anything, keeping it would have
      been a different flavor of the same dishonesty being fixed.

      **Dynamic Pop-ups**: new lib/marketing/popups.ts (full CRUD +
      `getActivePopupForStorefront()`), features/admin/marketing/
      popup-actions.ts gated on the real `popups.manage` permission,
      real image upload reusing the existing admin media pipeline
      (`uploadAdminMediaFiles(..., folder: "general")` — indexed in the
      same real MediaAsset table product images use, not a bespoke
      uploader). Rewrote admin-dynamic-popups.tsx for real list/CRUD
      (enable toggle, bulk enable/disable/delete, real per-popup delay
      field) and wired the previously-missing edit route
      (`/admin/marketing/popups/[id]`, which existed as a page file but
      still pointed at the mock array). **Actually renders on the
      storefront now** — new components/storefront/dynamic-popup.tsx
      mounted in app/(storefront)/layout.tsx next to the existing
      StorefrontAnalytics/StorefrontChatWidget (same real-widget
      pattern), showing the first enabled popup (by position, then
      oldest) after its configured delay, at most once per browser
      session (sessionStorage), with a real image/title/summary/button
      matching the admin preview exactly.

      **Custom Visitors**: per the operator's explicit choice of real
      tracking over a disclosed-synthetic number (matching this
      session's consistent no-fake-data approach). New lib/analytics/
      visitor-id.ts (anonymous per-browser cookie, written only inside
      Server Actions/Route Handlers per Next.js rules — never during
      Server Component render) and lib/analytics/product-views.ts
      (`recordProductView`, `getLiveViewerCount` — counts *distinct*
      viewer keys in a rolling window, so repeat views by the same
      visitor don't inflate the number). A new tiny client component
      (features/storefront/product-view-tracker.tsx) fires a
      fire-and-forget server action on PDP mount to record the view;
      the PDP page itself computes the live count server-side per
      request (no polling/websockets — accurate as of each page load,
      which is honest and sufficient, not over-engineered). Rewrote the
      admin page from the generic fake `AdminEngagementList` table into
      a real settings form (enable + counting-window minutes + minimum-
      to-show threshold) — new lib/marketing/visitor-widget-settings.ts,
      gated on `notifications.manage` (no dedicated "visitors" permission
      exists in the catalogue; this route was already gated on
      `notifications.view` before this task, so reused the matching
      manage-level key rather than inventing a new one).

      Verified against real Postgres with a temporary script: a popup
      stays hidden while disabled and appears from
      `getActivePopupForStorefront()` only once enabled, then disappears
      after delete; viewer counting correctly returns 2 for two distinct
      viewers even when one of them generated 2 view events (proves the
      DISTINCT-based counting, not a raw event count, which would have
      wrongly shown 3); invalid visitor-settings input (windowMinutes=0)
      is rejected without changing stored state. Also re-verified
      notification types/settings end-to-end (locked-type enable toggle,
      duplicate-name rejection, settings round-trip) since both shipped
      in this same task. All test rows cleaned up after. `tsc --noEmit`

      admin routes verified live with no 404/500.

      New components/storefront/site-alert.tsx renders the first enabled

      popup widgets.

      Also re-learned mid-task: after `prisma generate` regenerates the
      client, the *running* dev server process still has the old client
      loaded in memory and throws `Cannot read properties of undefined
      (reading 'findMany')` on the new model until restarted — hit this
      exact error live on `/admin/marketing/popups` between sessions
      (reported by the operator), fixed by restarting the dev server (no
      code change needed), and proactively restarted the server again
      right after this task's own migration/generate step to avoid a
      repeat. Worth remembering as standard procedure after every
      schema-adding task in this project, not just when a fresh
      migration happens to also need a data-loss confirmation prompt.

      Verified against real Postgres with a temporary script: alert
      stays hidden while disabled, becomes the active storefront alert
      once enabled with the exact chosen location/auto-close values,
      appears in the admin list, and both bulk-disable and delete work
      correctly. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run test:payments` 47/47,
      homepage/PDP/both touched admin routes verified live with no
      404/500 after the dev-server restart.
- [x] AD-260 Made Custom Sale Alert real, operator's direct follow-up on
      the last deferred-then-picked-up item from the AD-258 Marketing
      audit (/admin/marketing/sale-alerts). This one had a genuine
      privacy question the other Marketing items didn't: the settings
      screen is for a "someone just bought X" toast, and doing that with
      *real* purchase data means deciding how much of the real
      customer's identity to expose to anonymous storefront visitors.
      Resolved it by design rather than by asking: `Order` already has a
      real `shippingArea` relation (from the AD-254 District/Upazila
      work) with a ready-made human label (e.g. "Dhamrai — Dhaka"), so
      the toast shows real product + real shipping area + the real order
      date — and never the customer's name, email, or phone. This
      sidesteps the privacy trade-off entirely rather than needing an
      explicit "real vs anonymized" choice, so no AskUserQuestion was
      needed this round.

      Also resolved without asking: real recent orders vs a disclosed-
      synthetic feed (the same fork posed for AD-258 Custom Visitors).
      Unlike Visitors, real tracking infrastructure already exists in
      full here — `Order`/`OrderItem` are already real and queryable —
      so "real" was strictly less work than inventing a plausible fake
      feed, not a genuine trade-off worth pausing for.

      New SaleAlertSettings singleton (migration
      20260909053136_marketing_sale_alert_settings — applied cleanly,
      pure addition): enabled, min/max show interval seconds,
      productScope (featured/sale/manual), manualProductIds. New
      lib/marketing/sale-alerts.ts: `getSaleAlertSettings`/
      `saveSaleAlertSettings` (validates min ≤ max, both 5–3600s),
      `listPickableProducts` (for the manual-scope picker — deliberately
      built as a plain new real query rather than reusing the existing
      admin-promo-product-picker-modal.tsx, since that picker's props
      are still typed against a mock catalogue module, not real Product
      data), and `getRecentSaleAlertEvents()` — the core real query:
      recent non-cancelled `OrderItem`s (scoped to the top-20-by-position
      active products for "featured", `isSale: true` products for
      "sale", or the admin's manual product-id list), each mapped to
      `{ productName, productSlug, areaLabel, placedAt }` only — the
      Prisma `select` clause itself makes it structurally impossible to
      leak `Order.customerName`/`customerEmail`/`customerPhone` since
      they're never selected. features/admin/marketing/
      sale-alert-actions.ts gates saves on the real, already-seeded
      `alerts.manage` permission (same group as Custom Alerts).

      Rewrote AdminSaleAlertSettingsPage (same file as Custom Alerts)
      for real save, plus a real searchable product checklist for the
      "manual" scope (only rendered when that scope is selected) sourced
      from `listPickableProducts()`. New components/storefront/
      sale-alert-toast.tsx renders the rotating toast on the storefront:
      cycles through the real event pool at a random interval between
      the admin's min/max seconds, shows each for 6s, links to the real
      product page. Relative time is computed honestly from the real
      order date (e.g. "3 days ago") — deliberately never fabricated as
      "just now" for an old real order, since that would reintroduce the

      Fixed bottom-left by convention; noted as a known minor limitation
      that it can visually stack with a Custom Alert also set to
      bottom-left (not solved — would need a shared stacking coordinator
      across three independent widgets, out of scope for this task).

      Verified against real Postgres with a temporary script: found 8

      created one throwaway real order with a deliberately suspicious

- [x] AD-261 Made Email Templates and the Newsletter campaign gap real,
      per the operator's explicit "make these professionally dynamic" on
      both /admin/marketing/email-templates and /admin/newsletter.
      Newsletter's subscriber management (subscribe/unsubscribe) was

      Two real scope decisions resolved by design rather than by asking,
      since the reasoning was clear-cut in both cases:
      1. **Whether to wire templates into real order-lifecycle sends
         now.** Kept to the same disclosed-gap pattern already validated
         for NotificationTypeSetting (AD-258): real CRUD +  a real "Send
         test email" button (using the existing lib/mail/send.ts SMTP
         sender, same as OTP/SMTP test-sends), but not yet triggered
         automatically by order events — no code creates an email keyed
         to one of these today, and wiring all ~8 real order-lifecycle
         trigger points is a materially bigger, separate task that
         touches checkout/order-processing code. Disclosed explicitly in
         the admin UI copy itself, not just in this log.
      2. **Whether "send a campaign" is safe to build for real.** Yes —
         reused the exact same real SMTP sender already used for
         Contacts replies and SMTP/OTP test-sends, so no new send
         mechanism was invented. The real risk (an actual externally-
         visible, unrecoverable email blast) was handled by NOT
         triggering any real send during my own verification — confirmed
         `SMTP_PASSWORD` is unset in this dev environment first, so the
         sender fails closed before ever opening a network connection,
         letting the full code path (including real per-recipient
         success/failure counting) be exercised safely.

      status (draft/sending/sent/failed), recipientCount/sentCount/
      failedCount/lastError — all real, measured outcomes, never assumed
      successful.

      lib/mail/templates.ts: real CRUD (subject/body/enabled — emailType/

      support anywhere** (the schema had `BlogPost.coverMediaId` all
      along, but no admin upload UI, no server action, and no storefront
      display ever used it — a real image field sitting completely
      unwired), and the per-post metadata was real but partial — plain
      `title`/`description` only, no Open Graph image/type/canonical URL,
      no Twitter card, and no structured data at all (this app has zero
      JSON-LD anywhere, not just on blog — scoped to fixing it for blog
      only, as asked, not retrofitting product pages too).

      Wired the cover image for real: new `uploadBlogCoverImageAction` in
      blog-actions.ts reuses the existing real admin media pipeline
      (`uploadAdminMediaFiles(..., folder: "general")` — same real
      MediaAsset table product/popup/alert images already use, not a new
      uploader). Added `coverMediaId` to `savePost`'s real update/create
      data and `coverImagePath`/`coverMediaId` to the read side
      (`postInclude` now also selects the `coverMedia` relation).
      Deliberately exposed BOTH the resolved display path
      (`coverImagePath`, for rendering) and the raw FK
      (`coverMediaId`, for round-tripping on save) on the admin type —
      without the latter, saving unrelated field changes would have
      silently wiped a post's existing cover image on every edit, since
      the form only ever sees the resolved path, not the id. Verified
      this exact failure mode doesn't happen (see below). Admin form
      gained a real upload-on-select field with a live preview; cover
      images now render on both the storefront blog list (as a card
      thumbnail) and the post page (full-width above the excerpt).

      Built the "full SEO system" pieces: both blog pages now set
      `alternates.canonical` via the existing `publicOrigin()` helper
      (same one the sitemap already uses); the post page's
      `generateMetadata` now sets real `openGraph` (type: "article",
      the real cover image, `publishedTime`) and `twitter` card metadata,
      mirroring the exact pattern the product page already uses for
      its own OG tags — not a new convention. Added real BlogPosting
      JSON-LD structured data (headline/description/image/datePublished/
      author/publisher) rendered as a `<script type="application/ld+json">`
      tag on the post page — genuinely new to this codebase (nothing
      anywhere had structured data before), scoped to blog only since
      that's what was asked.

      Verified against real Postgres end-to-end: uploaded a real 1×1 PNG
      through the real media pipeline, created a real published post
      with that cover + real SEO title/description, and confirmed it
      appears immediately in both `getPublicPost()` and
      `listPublicPosts()` with the correct cover path, SEO fields, and

      either public query; confirmed re-saving the post with its
      *existing* `coverMediaId` (simulating an admin editing text without
      touching the image) does not wipe the cover — the exact regression
      the dual id/path exposure above was built to prevent. Hit one
      false alarm along the way: a public-list check appeared to fail
      because my own test script compared against an un-normalized slug
      (the real `normalizeSlug()` correctly stripped a leading
      underscore I'd used for test-naming) — confirmed via a direct
      Prisma query that the real function was correct all along, not a
      product bug. Also incidentally re-confirmed `BlogPost.coverMediaId
      onDelete: SetNull` works as designed: deleting the test
      MediaAsset (during my own cleanup) correctly nulled the
      already-orphaned post's cover reference rather than leaving a
      dangling FK or erroring.

      Confirmed live on the real running site: real JSON-LD, real
      `og:title`/`og:description`/`og:type=article`, and a real
      `<link rel="canonical">` all present on the existing real

      Chose NOT to install literal Summernote — it requires jQuery,
      which nothing else in this Next.js 16 / React 19 app uses, has
      shakier modern React/SSR integration, and is far less actively
      maintained than modern alternatives. Installed **TipTap v3**
      (`@tiptap/react`, `@tiptap/starter-kit`, `@tiptap/extension-link`,
      `@tiptap/extension-placeholder`) instead — a headless, actively
      maintained, React-native rich-text editor that delivers the same
      real toolbar experience (bold/italic/strike/headings/lists/quote/
      code/link/undo-redo) the operator was asking for, without a jQuery
      dependency. Also installed `sanitize-html` (+ `@types/sanitize-html`).

      This is a genuinely higher-risk change than prior UI-only work: the
      Body field moves from plain text to real HTML, which means (1) a
      migration-safe rendering path for the ~2 existing plain-text posts,
      and (2) a real XSS surface that needs closing, since the stored
      value now becomes real markup rendered via `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`
      on the live storefront. Handled both:
      - New lib/content/sanitize-html.ts: `sanitizeBlogBody()` (real
        server-side allowlist sanitizer — strips `<script>`, event-handler
        attributes, and `javascript:` URLs; keeps only genuine formatting
        tags/attributes) and `looksLikeHtml()` (detects real markup vs.
        legacy plain text). `savePost` in lib/content/blog.ts now calls
        `sanitizeBlogBody()` on every save — the real security boundary,
        independent of whatever the client/editor sent, matching this
        session's "trust the server function, not the client" pattern
        used everywhere else (same posture as B2B document validation,
        payment amount checks, etc.).
      - The storefront post page now renders `looksLikeHtml(body)` ?
        sanitized HTML via `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` : the original
        `\n\n`-split paragraph rendering — so the two real existing posts
        (plain text, pre-dating this change) keep rendering correctly
        with zero migration needed, while new rich-text posts render
        their real formatting.
      - New features/admin/marketing/rich-text-editor.tsx wraps TipTap
        with a real toolbar; new shared `.th-rich-text` CSS block in
        app/globals.css styles headings/lists/blockquotes/links/code
        identically in the admin editor and the live storefront page, so
        what the admin sees while writing matches what actually renders
        (same principle already applied to the SEO preview in AD-263).
      - Body is no longer a native form control end-to-end (TipTap owns
        its own contentEditable surface), so the "required" check moved
        from the browser's native validation to an explicit
        `stripHtml(body).trim()` check in the submit handler — word count
        also now strips tags before counting so it reflects real words,
        not markup characters.

      **Did not just build it** — asked the operator first
      (AskUserQuestion), since the real design question isn't whether to
      sanitize the content (sanitizing raw `<script>` tags would defeat
      the entire feature; third-party tracking/chat snippets are
      supposed to run) but *who* gets to save it: any staff account with
      page access could otherwise silently plant a skimmer or
      session-stealer that runs for every real storefront visitor.

      reuse the existing broader Marketing Analytics permission the same
      staff already use for SEO/GA4/etc. Operator chose the dedicated
      Admin-only permission.

      despite its name, is the real seed source `prisma/seed.ts` reads —
      not dead mock data) and to its `MANAGER_DENIED` set, matching the
      existing high-risk bucket (`staff.add`, `smtp.manage`,
      `business.manage`, etc.). Granted both permissions to the Admin

      did **not** re-run the full `prisma/seed.ts`, since auditing it
      showed several models (e.g. `RefundReason`) unconditionally
      overwrite fields back to their original mock defaults on every
      re-run; doing so risked silently reverting real operator
      customization accumulated across this entire session just to add

      `Permission` rows and the `role-admin` `RolePermission` grants,

      Support got zero. Also fixed the route gate in
      `lib/auth/admin-route-permissions.ts`, which had been left
      pointing at the unrelated, too-broad `seo.view` (a leftover from

      New `lib/analytics/custom-scripts.ts`
      (`getAdminCustomScripts`/`getStorefrontCustomScripts`/
      `saveCustomScripts`) — content is stored and read back verbatim,
      unsanitized, by design; a 20,000-character cap per field guards
      against accidental storage abuse only, not a security control.
      Audit log records only the header/footer **lengths**, never the
      raw script text, on every save. New gated "use server" action file
      (`features/admin/analytics/custom-script-actions.ts`) mirroring
      the existing `analytics-actions.ts` shape exactly (same-origin
      guard, permission check, actor-stamped audit log, revalidate).

      Real storefront injection went through two designs. First built a
      client-side approach (`"use client"` component rebuilding each
      `<script>` element via `document.createElement` after mount, since
      `.innerHTML` never executes injected `<script>` tags) — then
      replaced it with something simpler and more correct: a plain
      Server Component (`components/analytics/custom-script-slot.tsx`)
      using `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`. Content set that way becomes part
      of the *server-rendered* HTML the browser parses on initial load,
      so `<script>` tags inside it execute exactly like any other inline
      script written directly in the page — no client bundle, no DOM
      surgery, and (unlike the client version) directly verifiable with
      a plain HTTP fetch of the rendered page. Header slot renders near
      the top of the storefront layout, footer slot at the very end —
      this layout has no literal `<head>` of its own (nested inside the
      root layout's `<body>`), which matches this codebase's own existing
      precedent: real GTM/GA4/Meta Pixel scripts already inject via
      `next/script` from within the body tree, not a literal `<head>`,
      with no issue.

      **Hit the session's recurring client/server Prisma-leak bug shape
      again**, in a new variant: value-importing a server-only constant
      (`CUSTOM_SCRIPT_MAX` from `lib/analytics/custom-scripts.ts`) into
      the already-"use client" `admin-analytics-nexa-pages.tsx` pulled
      `pg`/`dns`/`fs`/`net`/`tls`/`util` into the browser bundle —
      surfaced immediately as `Module not found` errors in the dev
      server's own compile log after a routine post-migration restart
      (not by `test:payments`, since this only broke the specific
      `/admin/integrations/*` analytics pages, not the storefront
      homepage the payment-security HTTP check hits). Fixed the same way
      as B2B (AD-257) and EMI (AD-243) before it: mirrored the constant
      as a plain local value inside the client file instead of importing
      it. Notably, restarting the dev server alone did **not** clear the
      error the first time — Turbopack had a stale persistent cache in
      `.next` from the mid-edit compile that still referenced the bad
      import; deleting `.next` and restarting resolved it. Worth
      remembering: if a plain dev-server restart doesn't clear a
      module-not-found error that the current source no longer causes,
      clear `.next` before assuming it's a real remaining bug.

      Verified against real Postgres and the live dev server with a
      temporary script: real save/read round-trip byte-for-byte
      (including exact script markup), the audit log recorded the exact
      correct lengths (never the content itself), an over-length input
      was rejected with nothing written, and — the real end-to-end
      proof — a distinctive marker `<script>` saved through the real
      save function was then found byte-for-byte inside the **live
      homepage's actual HTML response**, fetched over real HTTP against
      the running dev server, not just confirmed in application state;
      cleared back to empty afterward. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run
      test:payments` 47/47, `eslint` clean on every touched file, live
      routes verified (`/admin/integrations/custom-script`,
      `/admin/integrations/meta-capi`, `/admin/seo` → 307 to login;
      homepage/checkout → 200).

      **This closes the entire AD-267 11-page Analytics/Integrations/SEO
      batch** — every page the operator listed together in one message
      is now genuinely real and connected to the live storefront: 8
      needed zero changes (confirmed by audit, not assumed), Facebook
      Catalog Products (AD-267) and Meta Conversion API (AD-268) gained
      new real functionality, and Custom Scripts (AD-269) gained a new
      real, deliberately access-gated feature. Per the operator's
      standing process instruction for this batch, holding here for
      final approval.
- [x] AD-270 Report Center, first pass (3 of 7 pages) — operator request
      for professional, zero-mistake, date-wise/month-wise accurate
      accounting reports across all 7 /admin/reports pages, with CSV/XLS
      export, given this is a single-vendor store.

      Audited all 7 pages first (research agent) rather than assume
      uniform scope. Found 6 fully mock (`AdminEarningReport`'s KPIs/
      charts, Product Sale, Wishlist, Searches, Wallet, Compare all used
      a `hash(id, N)` fake-number generator or hardcoded arrays — zero
      connection to real Order/OrderItem/Refund data despite that data
      being fully real and available); Stock was already half-real
      (`listAdminStockReportRows` in lib/catalog/admin-inventory.ts is
      live whenever `DATA_SOURCE!=mock`, which this app always requires).
      Also surfaced a fully dead, orphaned generic "Report List" system
      (`admin-report-list.tsx`/`load-reports.ts`/`reports-mock.ts` + the
      `/admin/reports/[id]` route) never linked from any real page —
      noted, not touched, separate matter.

      4 of the 7 pages could not be honestly made "real" as a same-shaped
      report fix — each needed a genuine scope decision, not a guess:
      **Wishlist**/**Compare** have real, completely unused Prisma models
      (`Wishlist`/`WishlistItem`, `CompareList`/`CompareListItem`) but the
      storefront features themselves are localStorage-only, explicitly
      labeled "not synced to an account yet" — a real report needs that
      synced first, which is a storefront feature change, not a report
      fix. **User Searches** has no tracking model anywhere in the schema
      — the storefront search page only ever reads the `q` param per
      request, nothing persists it. **Wallet Recharge History** is the
      deepest gap: no ledger exists (confirmed the AD-256 project-memory
      note is still accurate — `adjustCustomerWallet()` is a direct
      `walletAmount` field write inside a transaction, no history row),
      and there is no real customer-facing wallet top-up flow via bKash/
      Nagad/Card at all — the mock's own "recharge method" column has
      nothing real to source from.

      Asked the operator directly (AskUserQuestion, 4 questions) rather
      than guessing on any of these: (1) Wishlist/Compare — defer both
      with an honest "not available" state rather than building the
      bigger real-sync feature now; (2) Wallet — build a real
      WalletTransaction-shaped **adjustment ledger** (accurate, but
      honestly scoped to admin-initiated adjustments, not fake customer
      recharges) rather than the much bigger real-payment-gateway
      top-up flow; (3) export format — CSV only, no new `.xlsx`
      dependency; (4) sequencing — build the 3 straightforward
      real-data-already-exists pages now, then check in before touching
      Searches/Wallet/Wishlist/Compare. All 4 recommended options chosen.

      **Earning Report** (`lib/admin/load-report-center.ts::
      loadEarningReport`, fully rewritten): real double-entry-consistent
      accounting, not approximations. "Sales" = `Order` rows with
      `paymentStatus: PAID` only (an unpaid or cancelled order was never
      real revenue — the old mock summed ALL catalog products' prices
      regardless of whether they ever sold). "Refunds" = `Refund` rows
      with `status: COMPLETED` only (a requested-or-approved-but-not-
      paid-out refund hasn't actually left the business yet). The
      "Net Sales" breakdown (Product Sales / Delivery / Tax when
      nonzero) is built from the exact same fields that sum to
      `Order.totalAmount` (`subtotal − discount + shipping + tax`), so
      it is structurally incapable of drifting from the real total —
      replacing the old fake fixed 92/8 split. The "Expenses" breakdown
      now groups real refunds by the real `RefundChannel` enum (Wallet/
      Offline/Gateway) — a genuine, meaningful accounting split that
      already existed in the schema — replacing a fake fixed 65/35
      split that had no real per-refund category to draw from anyway.
      Added two new real KPIs the mock never had: paid order count and
      average order value, both period-scoped.

      New `lib/admin/report-time-buckets.ts` for real **date-wise and
      month-wise** grouping, per the operator's explicit ask: hourly
      buckets for Today (24 points), daily for Week (7) and Month
      (days-in-month), and real calendar-month buckets for All (last 12
      months) — all UTC-based, deliberately matching the existing
      month-start convention in `lib/admin/load-dashboard.ts` rather
      than introducing a second timezone convention just for this
      report. Real per-bucket sums come from `prisma.$queryRaw` using
      Postgres `date_trunc` (Prisma's `groupBy` cannot truncate a
      timestamp column, so raw SQL is the correct tool here, not a
      workaround) for both the sale and refund time series. Missing
      buckets are filled with real 0s (`fillBuckets`) rather than gaps,
      so a quiet hour/day/month reads honestly as zero. Also replaced
      the old arbitrary "first brand in the list" top-brand figure with
      a real revenue-ranked query (`OrderItem` → `Order` → `Product` →
      `Brand`, PAID orders only, scoped to the selected period).
      `LineChart` in `admin-report-center-ui.tsx` now takes labeled
      `{label,value}` points and shows real date/month labels (sampled
      to ~8 to stay readable across 7–31-point series) instead of bare
      point indices.

      **Product Sale Report** (`loadProductSaleRows`, rewritten): real
      units sold + real revenue per product via `OrderItem.groupBy`
      filtered to PAID orders (Prisma's relation-filtered groupBy —
      `where: { order: { paymentStatus: "PAID" } }` — confirmed to work
      directly, no raw SQL needed here). Every active catalog product is
      still listed even with zero real sales (shown as 0), matching the
      report's original full-catalog intent rather than only showing
      movers. Added a real "Revenue" column next to the existing unit
      count — extended `AdminCategoryReportTable` with an optional
      secondary column + a formatter prop, backward-compatible with
      Wishlist/Stock which don't set it.

      **Stock Report**: confirmed already real end-to-end (`available =
      quantity − reserved`, `Product.stockStatus` correctly denormalized)
      — no data-layer change needed, just added CSV export and a
      clearer "Available Stock" column header.

      **CSV export** (new `features/admin/reports/report-csv-export.tsx`,
      a generic `ReportCsvExportButton` mirroring the one existing CSV
      pattern in `admin-bulk-export.tsx` — Blob + object URL, no new
      dependency): Earning Report exports both a KPI-summary CSV and a
      full time-series CSV; Product Sale/Stock export **every real row**,
      not just the current page's slice — exporting a paginated slice
      would have produced a silently incomplete, misleading accounting
      export, which would be a real mistake given the operator's
      explicit "0 mistake hishab" requirement.

      Verified against real Postgres with a temporary before/after delta
      script (deleted after use — no schema change this pass, so no
      dev-server restart needed): inserted one real test `Order` (PAID,
      known subtotal=900/shipping=60/tax=0/total=960) with a real
      product-linked `OrderItem` (qty 3, revenue 900) and one real
      `COMPLETED` `Refund` (amount 200, channel GATEWAY), then confirmed
      the loader's output moved by **exactly** the right amount on every
      figure checked: total sales (+960), refunds (+200), paid order
      count (+1), the "Product Sales" net-sales line (+900), the
      "Delivery" net-sales line (+60), the "Gateway" expense line
      (+200), the correct real hour-bucket in both time series (+960 /
      +200), and the test product's real unit/revenue delta (+3 units /
      +900 revenue) — every check exact, not approximate. Deleted the
      test refund and order afterward and confirmed the report returned
      to its **exact** original baseline (not just "close"). Also
      smoke-tested all 4 period tabs for correct bucket counts (24/7/30/
      12) and real, correctly-formatted date/month labels. `tsc --noEmit`
      clean, `npm run test:payments` 47/47, `eslint` clean on every
      touched file, all 3 report routes verified live (307 to login
      unauthenticated; homepage unaffected at 200).

      **Holding here for operator check-in** before the remaining 4
      pages (User Searches, Wallet Adjustment Ledger, and the deferred-
      with-honest-disclosure treatment for Wishlist/Compare) — per the
      agreed sequencing from the AskUserQuestion above.
- [x] AD-270 Report Center, second pass (final 4 of 7 pages) — operator
      approved continuing straight through rather than stopping after
      the check-in.

      **User Searches**: new `SearchLog` model, storing `query`
      lowercased/trimmed at write time so `groupBy` produces correct
      counts without a separate normalization step at read time. New
      `lib/search/log-search.ts::recordSearchQuery()` — fire-and-forget,
      wrapped in try/catch so a logging failure can never break real
      search results (same non-fatal-wrapper posture as
      `sendMailSafe`/`sendMetaCapiPurchaseSafe`). Wired into the one
      real place a search actually happens:
      `features/catalog/search-listing.tsx`, right after the existing
      real `productRepository.list()` call, logging the real result
      count alongside the query. `loadUserSearches()` now reads real
      `prisma.searchLog.groupBy` ordered by count. Disclosed
      simplification, not a bug: every search request is logged
      unconditionally (no per-visitor/per-session dedup), so repeatedly
      searching the same term inflates its count same as distinct
      searchers would — acceptable for "what are people searching",
      revisit only if "unique searchers" specifically is wanted later.

      Verified by hitting the **real live `/search` route over real
      HTTP** with a distinctive marker query string (not just calling
      the lib function in isolation) and confirming a real `SearchLog`
      row landed and `loadUserSearches()` counted it correctly, then
      deleting the marker rows and confirming the report returned to
      its exact prior state.

      **Wallet Adjustment Ledger** (renamed everywhere it appears — nav
      label, page `<title>`, report heading — from "Wallet Recharge
      History", since it never was recharges and the old name actively
      misled): new `WalletTransaction` model plus a real
      `User.walletTransactions` relation. Extended the existing
      `adjustCustomerWallet()` (`lib/admin/save-customer.ts`, AD-256) to
      write a real ledger row — signed amount, resulting balance,
      optional free-text reason, and which staff member made it — inside
      the **same** `$transaction` as the balance update itself, so the
      ledger is structurally incapable of drifting from the real
      balance (no separate write that could partially fail). Added a
      second `window.prompt()` for an optional reason on the existing
      wallet-adjust row action in
      `admin-customer-row-actions.tsx` — deliberately kept to the same
      "simple prompt, no new dialog" scope the operator chose for AD-256,
      not upgraded into a bigger form now that a ledger exists. New
      `loadWalletLedger()` reads real transactions joined to the real
      customer (name/email), formatted consistently with the rest of the
      app's date formatting.

      Verified against a real, already-existing customer row (not a
      throwaway test user): a real +777 adjustment produced a ledger row
      with the exact right amount/balance-after/reason/staff-email,
      confirmed the real `User.walletAmount` matched exactly, then
      issued a real -777 reversal, deleted both test ledger rows, and
      confirmed the customer's real wallet balance returned to its
      **exact** original value (0), not just "close."

      **Wishlist / Compare**: per the operator's earlier explicit
      decision (AskUserQuestion, prior pass), deferred honestly rather
      than built. `loadWishlistRows()` now always returns
      `available: false` (the real `Wishlist`/`WishlistItem` models
      still exist and are still completely unused — nothing changed
      there, only the report's honesty); its `page.tsx` renders a plain
      `EmptyState` explaining exactly why (storefront wishlist is
      localStorage-only, not synced to accounts) instead of the old fake
      per-product `hash()` counts. `AdminCompareReport` was replaced
      entirely — deleted the inline `MOCK_COMPARE_ROWS` array and its
      client-side search/filter UI, replaced with the same honest
      `EmptyState` pattern, and simplified from a `"use client"`
      component to a plain server-renderable one since there's no
      interactive state left to justify the client boundary.

      Removed now-fully-dead code after confirming zero remaining
      references anywhere: `MOCK_USER_SEARCHES`, `MOCK_WALLET_RECHARGES`,
      and the `WalletRechargeRow` type from
      `lib/admin/report-center-mock.ts`.

      Hit two false alarms while verifying, both recognized and resolved
      correctly rather than chased as real bugs: (1) ran `tsc --noEmit`
      while `npm run dev` was still live from the prior phase, which
      corrupted `.next/dev/types/routes.d.ts` into nonsense syntax
      errors — this is the session's own documented dev-server/build
      conflict (see Pending Decisions in PROJECT_STATE.md), fixed by
      stopping the dev server and clearing `.next`, not by debugging the
      fake errors. (2) `npm run test:payments` briefly reported 45/47
      checks — not a regression: 2 of the 47 checks make real HTTP calls
      to the live app (`scripts/payments/check-security.ts` hits
      `${origin}/api/payments/sslcommerz/ipn`), and the dev server was
      down at that moment from fix (1); restarting it brought the count
      back to the real 47/47, confirmed rather than assumed.

      Final verification: `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run test:payments`
      47/47 (with the dev server correctly running), `eslint` clean
      (one intentionally-harmless unused-parameter warning on
      `loadWishlistRows`'s now-inert category filter, kept so a real
      implementation can drop in later without changing the call site),
      all 7 `/admin/reports/*` routes verified live (307 to login
      unauthenticated), homepage/checkout/search all unaffected (200).

      **This closes the entire AD-270 Report Center task.** Final state
      across all 7 pages: 5 genuinely real end-to-end (Earning, Product
