- [x] AD-274 Real product bulk CSV import + export at
      /admin/catalog/import and /admin/catalog/export — operator
      request: a properly designed CSV with real fields for bulk
      import, matched by a real export.

      Audited both first (research agent): **Import was 100% mock** —
      `handleUpload` in the old `admin-bulk-import.tsx` never read the
      uploaded file at all, just toasted "queued (mock)"; its template
      CSV used fake numeric `unit_id`/`attribute_ids`/
      `warranty_note_id` columns matching no real schema field or the
      real product-save function's actual input shape. **Export was
      already real** (a separate route) but covered only 12 of ~20
      relevant fields, and — a genuine bug found while investigating,
      not assumed — silently capped at 48 rows: it requested
      `pageSize: 500` from `productRepository.list()`, but that shared
      storefront-listing repository clamps every request to its own
      `MAX_PAGE_SIZE = 48` internally. Harmless today (real catalog ≈26
      products) but would have silently truncated every export once the
      catalog passed 48 products — fixed as part of this task, not
      left for later.

      **Import**: added `papaparse` — the codebase's first real CSV
      *parser*. Every prior CSV feature this session only ever *wrote*
      CSV (hand-rolled `escapeCsv`); parsing multi-line CSV correctly
      (quoted fields, embedded commas/newlines) is a genuinely different
      and harder problem, not worth hand-rolling against a well-known
      library. New `lib/catalog/bulk-csv.ts` reuses the *exact* real
      single-product validation/persistence path per row
      (`saveAdminProduct` → `parseProductInput` → `persistParsed`) — no
      parallel write logic invented. Real semantics: **SKU is the match
      key** (existing SKU → update, new SKU → create), each row
      independently wrapped in try/catch so one bad row can't abort the
      batch, and the UI shows a real per-row results table (row #, SKU,
      name, created/updated/failed, error text). This per-row-report UX
      is genuinely new to this codebase — checked every other "bulk"
      feature here (customers, alerts, popups, notifications) and all of
      them are checkbox-select + single aggregate result, not
      file-upload-with-per-row-report, so there was no existing pattern
      to mirror, only the general server-action-gating shape to reuse.

      **Deliberately scoped down, disclosed directly in the import
      page's own instructions, not silently missing**: bulk CSV covers
      flat/core fields only (name, brand, category, pricing, stock,
      position, weight, is_active/new/sale, warranty label, YouTube
      link, one image, overview). Variants, colors, attributes, and spec
      groups are explicitly NOT covered — nested per-row structures that
      don't map onto flat CSV rows without a materially bigger design
      (multi-row-per-product or JSON-in-cell); the UI tells the operator
      to use the single-product editor for those instead of pretending
      bulk CSV handles everything. `brand`/`category` columns are real
      slugs (matching what `parseProductInput` actually expects), not
      the old mock's fake numeric IDs.

      **Found and fixed a real, directly-relevant gap while building
      this**: `weightGrams` (added in AD-254, feeds the real weight-
      based shipping rate) was never actually settable through
      `saveAdminProduct` — `ProductInputFields`/`parseProductInput`/
      `persistParsed` had no field for it at all, so even the
      single-product admin form could only ever leave it at the schema
      default (500g). Caught this directly during verification (the
      first real end-to-end test showed a submitted 777 came back as
      500). This was an already-disclosed AD-254 gap ("per-product
      editing not yet in admin-product-form.tsx"); closing it end-to-end
      (new `parseProductWeightGrams()` validator, threaded through both
      the update path's shared `productData()` builder and the separate
      literal create-data block in `admin-products.ts`) was small,
      directly served bulk import's own correctness, and now benefits
      the single-product save path too — though that form still has no
      UI input for the field yet (disclosed as a smaller separate
      follow-up, not built here).

      **Export**: `listAllProductsForExport()` queries Prisma directly
      instead of going through the capped storefront repository — same
      "admin bulk reads go straight to Prisma" pattern already
      established by the Report Center loaders (AD-270). Real cap
      10,000 rows (documented, generous for a single-vendor catalog).
      Export and import now share the *exact same* `BULK_CSV_HEADERS`
      column set and order, so an exported file can be edited and
      re-imported directly — genuinely round-trippable, not just
      superficially similar-looking.

      Verified against real Postgres: a real create checked
      byte-for-byte against the database row afterward across ~15
      fields (including the newly-real `weightGrams`); a second import
      of the same file with one changed price correctly produced an
      **update**, not a duplicate row (confirmed via a real row-count
      check before/after); two deliberately invalid rows (missing
      brand, an unknown brand slug) correctly reported as failed with
      honest per-row error text (`"Select a brand."`, `"That brand no
      longer exists."`) without aborting the other rows in the same
      file; real export row count matched the real database product
      count exactly (no cap-related truncation); the exported row's
      every value exactly matched the database; the export CSV text
      contained the real test product's real data. Cleaned up the test
      product afterward. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run test:payments`
      47/47, `eslint` clean, `/admin/catalog/import`,
      `/admin/catalog/export`, `/admin/products`, homepage, and checkout
      all verified live (307 to login on the admin routes, confirmed via
      direct curl after Node's `fetch` in the verification script
      returned an unexpectedly cache-y 200 once — investigated
      immediately rather than assumed away, and confirmed it was a
      script artifact, not a real auth-gating gap).
- [x] AD-275 Made the 3 admin PC Builder pages real
      (/admin/pc-builder, /rules, /builds) — operator request.

      Audited all 3 first — a genuine mixed bag, not uniform mock:

      **Compatibility rules — already real, confirmed rather than
      assumed, then left alone.** Real `PCCompatibilityRule` model;
      toggling a rule type genuinely changes what the live storefront's
      `evaluateCompatibility()` checks via `listEnabledRuleTypes()`. No
      work needed — worth stating explicitly since it would have been
      easy to just rebuild all 3 pages uniformly without checking.

      **Saved builds — the same bug shape as AD-267's Facebook Catalog
      Products**: the real storefront save-a-build flow
      (`lib/pc-builder/saved-builds.ts`) already writes real
      `PCBuild`/`PCBuildItem` rows for signed-in customers, but the
      admin page imported `MOCK_PC_BUILDER_BUILDS` directly and showed 5
      fake rows ("Rahim Uddin", "Sadia Khan"...) regardless of what was
      actually in the database. Fixed with a real loader
      (`lib/pc-builder/admin-builds.ts::getAdminPcBuilderBuilds()`)
      reading the same `PCBuild` table — real owner label (customer
      name, or "Guest (not signed in)" for guest builds, which are
      device-local only and genuinely can't appear here until the
      customer signs in and saves), real component count via
      `_count.items`, a real share link only when a real `shareSlug`
      exists, and a real featured-toggle save action
      (`PCBuild.isFeatured` already existed in schema — no migration
      needed for that field). Deleted the now-fully-dead
      `MOCK_PC_BUILDER_BUILDS`/`getPcBuilderBuildById`/
      `AdminPcBuilderBuild` exports after confirming zero remaining
      references — kept `MOCK_PC_BUILDER_RULES` in the same file since
      it's still legitimately used as the real rules page's
      `DATA_SOURCE=mock` fallback.

      **Overview/Settings — the one genuine scope fork, asked rather
      than assumed.** "Enable PC Builder" + per-slot enabled/required
      toggles had zero backing schema. The real question wasn't mock-vs-
      real-save, it was whether real settings should also change what
      customers see: the live storefront slot list
      (`lib/domain/pc-builder/slots.ts`'s `BUILDER_SLOTS`) is a
      deliberately pure, synchronous module imported directly by ~5
      real, already-tested, working storefront files (slot list,
      part-picker sidebar, the `[slot]` select route, cart conversion,
      sharing) — making it DB-driven would mean converting that shared
      pure module to async everywhere it's used, real regression risk
      to a working, tested feature. Asked the operator: persist-only
      (matching the scope choice already made for Currency format/
      Feature Flags/Watermark) vs. apply live. **Chose persist-only.**

      New `PcBuilderSettings` (singleton) + `PcBuilderSlotConfig` (one
      real row per actual `BuilderSlot` enum value — all 11 real slots,
      not the old mock's approximate 8 with wrong ids like a generic
      "storage"/"cooler" that don't match the real
      `ssd`/`hdd`/`cpu_cooler`/`case_fans` split). Real save actions
      gated on `pc_builder.manage` — a permission key the audit found
      already defined in `feature-permissions-mock.ts` but never
      actually referenced by any route or action until now, so this is
      its first real use, not a new permission invented. UI copy states
      plainly, right on the same page, that these settings don't yet
      affect the live storefront — sitting directly next to the real,
      live-affecting Rules tab so the distinction is visible in context,
      not just recorded in a memory file the operator won't see.

      Verified against real Postgres: settings save/read round-tripped
      exactly (disabled the master toggle, disabled and un-required the
      monitor slot, confirmed both real via a fresh read), an unknown
      slot id was correctly rejected, everything restored to its exact
      original state afterward; a real test `PCBuild` (real user, real
      product, real `PCBuildItem`) was created directly in Postgres,
      confirmed the admin loader surfaced it with the exact right owner
      label / component count / status, toggled it featured through the
      real action and confirmed `isFeatured` actually flipped in the
      database, cleaned up after. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run
      test:payments` 47/47, **`npm run test:pc-builder` 38/38** (the
      existing pure compatibility-engine test suite — confirms zero
      regression to the untouched real engine), `eslint` clean, all 3
      admin routes plus the live storefront `/pc-builder` route
      verified via direct curl (307 / 200 respectively).
- [x] AD-276 Built the real PC Builder compatibility system — operator
      request (Banglish, explicit "analyze first" instruction): are all
      components tagged into slots, and build a real system so picking a
      motherboard suggests only genuinely compatible parts end to end
      (product tagging → live build selection → order traceability).
      Delivered a written analysis first per instruction, built only
      after explicit go-ahead ("full sytem ta kivabe build kora jay?").

      **Analysis (research agent traced real data flow before any
      code):** compatibility checking was only a post-hoc warning/hard-
      block on the review step, never a live filter while picking parts;
      real product tagging was sparse (14/25 products); no order/cart
      ever recorded which lines belonged to which build; a real
      `storageInterface` attribute already existed in the pure domain
      layer with zero Prisma column behind it. Planned as 3 steps: data
      foundation, live filtering, order/cart traceability.

      **Step 1 — data foundation.** Real `Product.builderStorageInterface`
      column (migration `20260909155028_pc_builder_compat_and_
      traceability`), wired through the full dual write-site chain
      (select blocks, `productData()` update block, the separate create
      block, mappers, repository) — same dual-create/update pattern as
      AD-274's `weightGrams` fix. New controlled-vocabulary constants
      (`lib/domain/pc-builder/attribute-options.ts`) for socket/RAM
      type/form factor/storage interface, each with an "Other…" escape
      hatch in the admin product form so uncommon real values still
      work; server validation stays length-only (not a hard allowlist)
      so "Other" is never blocked. Bulk CSV import still excludes
      builder attrs (unchanged AD-274 decision).

      **Step 2 — live suggest-as-you-pick filtering.** New pure
      `rankCandidatesForSlot()` reuses the existing tested
      `evaluateCompatibility()` engine: builds a hypothetical parts
      array (selected parts + candidate), evaluates it, keeps only
      warnings touching the target slot — an unrelated mismatch
      elsewhere can never hide an otherwise-fine candidate (dedicated
      test case confirms this). `/pc-builder/select/[slot]` (client-
      side, since selection lives in `useBuilderStore`/localStorage, not
      server/URL state) now fetches real selected parts + real enabled
      rule types via the existing `loadCompatibilityParts`/
      `loadEnabledRuleTypes` actions, ranks every candidate, hides
      definite mismatches by default behind a real toggle — "unknown"
      (missing data) is never hidden, matching the engine's existing
      never-claim-false-compatibility philosophy.

      **Step 3 — order/cart traceability.** `buildBatchId` (one
      `randomUUID()` per "Add build to cart" action) + `builderSlot`
      added to `CartItem` and `OrderItem` — a soft/informational tag,
      not an FK to `PCBuild` (saved builds are independently prunable).
      New `addBuildItemsToCart()` writes the shared batch id per line;
      `create-order.ts` copies both fields straight through cart→order
      (same pattern as the existing `colorName`/`colorHex` copy). Admin
      order detail shows a "PC Build" badge on lines that came from a
      build.

      Verified against real Postgres: extended `scripts/pc-builder/
      check-engine.ts` from 38→**44 checks** (6 new, all passing);
      `builderStorageInterface` round-tripped through a real product
      save; `rankCandidatesForSlot` correctly ranked synthetic RAM
      candidates against a real catalog motherboard's real `ramType`
      (match → ok, mismatch → incompatible); `addCartItemForOwner`'s new
      `buildMeta` param wrote the exact right `buildBatchId`/
      `builderSlot` to real `CartItem` rows for a real user; all test
      data cleaned up and confirmed removed. The `CartItem`→`OrderItem`
      copy-through was validated by code inspection + clean `tsc` rather
      than a full live order placement, because all 3 real
      `ShippingMethod` rows are still `isActive=false` (pre-existing,
      unrelated AD-254 condition, not touched) — `placeCustomerOrderForUser`
      hard-requires a shipping method, so no order can be placed at all
      right now regardless of this change. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint`
      clean on every touched file, `npm run test:payments` 47/47, live
      routes checked (`/pc-builder/select/motherboard` and `/select/ram`
      → 200; the 3 admin PC Builder routes → 307 auth redirect).

      **Disclosed, explicitly out of scope:** real product builder-slot
      tagging is still sparse (14/25 products) — a data-entry task for
      the operator, not something this code change fixes; suggestions
      will only improve as more real products carry real builder
      attribute values.

      (Today/Week/Month/All) and brand-filter chips had no `onClick` at

      **Data layer** (`lib/admin/load-dashboard.ts` + `dashboard-types.ts`
      rewritten): real 14-day daily paid-sales trend (reused the
      existing Report Center bucketing helpers — `planCustomRangeBuckets`/
      `fillBuckets`, AD-270 — instead of a second bucketing
      implementation); real top-5 categories/brands by revenue via raw
      SQL joins (`OrderItem`→`Order`→`Product`→`Category`/`Brand`, same
      join pattern as Report Center's `topBrand` query) with a real

      names on `topProducts`; real month-over-month growth percent
      (null / "New this month" instead of a misleading 0% when last
      month had no sales); real average order value; real new-customers-
      this-month count. Removed the dead "Confirmed order" status row —

      permanently stuck at 0. Removed redundant duplicate type fields
      (`store`, `topCategories`, `topBrands`, `brandChips`,
      `brandProducts`) after confirming via grep nothing outside the
      dashboard's own files referenced them.

      **UI layer:** new real inline-SVG `AdminTrendChart` (no chart
      library added — matches the codebase's existing lightweight
      custom-bar convention in the analytics feature) and a real
      `AdminDeltaBadge` ("+X% vs last month" / "New this month").
      Consolidated 3 duplicate top-products tables into one real table
      with real category names. Replaced the fake brand-chip filter and
      decorative period tabs with real content instead: a dedicated
      Revenue trend card, a real Recent orders table (order/customer/
      placed/payment+fulfillment badges/total — data the loader already
      computed but the old UI never showed), and real proportional
      revenue bars for top categories/brands. Deleted the now-unused
      `AdminPeriodTabs` export after confirming nothing else imported it.

      Verified against real Postgres: a cross-check script independently
      re-queried customer/product/order totals and the paid-revenue sum
      via separate direct Prisma calls and confirmed every figure in the
      snapshot matched exactly; confirmed the trend always returns
      exactly 14 buckets; confirmed every `sharePercent` is a real 0–100
      value; confirmed every `topProducts` row now has a real category
      instead of "—". `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean on all 4
      touched files, `/admin` still correctly 307-redirects signed-out.
      **Disclosed boundary:** no authenticated in-browser visual check —
      no browser automation tool is available in this environment, and
      replicating a Next.js server-action staff login purely via curl
      was judged not worth it versus the redirect check + independent
      real-data cross-check; same verification boundary already used for
      every other admin-only page this session.

- [x] AD-285 Storefront visual polish — operator: keep the design
      structure exactly as-is, but make it look more professional /
      attractive. Hard constraint: no structural change.

      Because structure was off-limits, all the work went into the
      design-token layer plus the most-repeated shared components, so
      every page lifts at once with nothing moving. No markup added,
      removed, or reordered anywhere.

      Tokens (`globals.css`): shadows were the main culprit — both were
      single flat washes, which is what made surfaces read as pasted-on;
      replaced with layered contact+ambient shadows and added
      `--shadow-lg` for hover elevation. Radii 6/10/16 → 8/12/20 (6px
      corners read boxy/dated). Found a real defect: `--color-primary-soft`
      was byte-identical to `--color-surface-muted` — a neutral gray, so
      every "soft primary" surface rendered dead gray instead of a brand
      tint; now a genuine teal tint. Added body font smoothing and
      heading tracking. Deliberately left `--color-primary`/`--color-
      secondary` alone — brand identity, and operator-overridable from
      Design Studio, so changing defaults would be off-brief and liable
      to be overridden anyway.

      Shared components (classes only, same props/markup): `Button` got
      real elevation + an `active:translate-y-px` press (was flat with
      only `transition-colors`); `controlClassName` (every Input/
      Textarea) got hover + focus-border feedback it completely lacked,
      while deliberately keeping the global `:focus-visible` outline
      rather than swapping in a prettier ring that would have weakened
      keyboard a11y; `HomeSectionHeader` got a primary accent bar via
      `border-l` on the existing wrapper (no new element) and a pill
      hover on its action link; `ProductCard` swapped its one-off
      arbitrary hover shadow for the new `shadow-lg` token; `SiteHeader`
      got a shadow so it separates from the page.

      `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean. Verified live: `/`, `/shop`,
      `/complaint` all 200, no server errors, and confirmed each change
      reaches the browser by grepping both the rendered HTML and the
      compiled Tailwind CSS. Worth remembering: Tailwind v4 emits shadow
      utilities via `--tw-shadow:` declarations, not literal
      `.shadow-lg{…}` selectors — grepping for the selector looks like a
      false miss.
- [x] AD-286 Header "PC Builder" CTA — operator: make the button
      beautiful, nice hover effect, a bit more modern.

      Its hover was backwards: `hover:bg-secondary/80` dimmed the copper
      on hover, so the CTA got quieter when it should get louder.
      Replaced with a sweeping white sheen across the button (~620ms), a
      shadow tinted with the button's own copper instead of neutral
      black, and a motion-safe lift. Fixed a real bug too: the
      active-route state used `ring-offset-surface`, which assumes a
      white backdrop — but the button sits on the dark navy header, so
      it drew a pale gap. Now an inset white ring, correct on both the
      dark header and the light mobile-nav sheet (its two usages).

      Two Tailwind-v4 gotchas, both caught by checking the compiled CSS
      rather than trusting the build:
      (1) `bg-[linear-gradient(…)]` / `bg-[length:…]` / `bg-[position:…]`
      are silently dropped in v4 — no error, classes present in the HTML
      but absent from the stylesheet. Rewrote the sheen as a real
      `.th-cta-sheen` class in `globals.css` (matching the existing
      `.th-*` convention).
      (2) `cn()` is a plain join with **no tailwind-merge**, so the
      shared variant's `hover:bg-secondary/90` can't be overridden from
      the class list. Pinned `background-color` inside `.th-cta-sheen` —
      unlayered CSS beats Tailwind's layered utilities.

      `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean. Verified in the compiled
      stylesheet: the sheen rule + hover sweep, both copper glow tints,
      and the inset ring all emit; `/` and `/pc-builder` both 200, active
      ring present on the builder route, no server errors. Future-grep
      note: dev CSS is pretty-printed, so `grep -o "\.class{[^}]*}"`
      false-misses multi-line rules — use `grep -A`.
- [x] AD-287 Homepage service bar + Top categories — operator: make
      these two sections prettier, more modern and eye-catching.

      Found a real bug behind why the category cards felt cheap: the
      card had `hover:shadow-md` + `hover:-translate-y-0.5` but its
      transition was `transition-colors`, so the lift and shadow snapped
      instantly with no animation. Widened the transition to cover
      transform/shadow/border/background.

      Both sections' icon chips were a flat `bg-primary/10` wash — pale
      and lifeless. Gave both the same chip language so they read as
      siblings: `bg-gradient-to-br from-primary/15 to-primary/5` for
      depth, a `ring-1 ring-primary/10` edge, and on hover a rich
      gradient fill (`from-primary to-primary-hover`) with a
      primary-tinted glow (not neutral black) plus a slight scale. Card
      hover lifts further with `shadow-lg` and a `primary-soft` tint —
      that token being a real teal tint now, since AD-285 fixed it from
      the dead grey it used to be. Kept a flat `bg-primary/10`
      background-colour under the gradient background-image as a
      fallback so a chip can never render as a transparent hole.

      `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean. Verified in the compiled CSS:
      gradient direction, `color-mix` stops driven by `--color-primary`,
      ring colours, and both glow tints all emit; `/` 200, no errors.
      **Tailwind v4 grep note (third time bitten):** v4 emits gradients
      as `background-image: linear-gradient(var(--tw-gradient-stops))`
      plus `--tw-gradient-position: to bottom right in oklab` — grepping
      for a literal `linear-gradient(to bottom right` returns 0 and looks
      like total failure while the utility works fine. Check
      `--tw-gradient-position` / `--tw-gradient-from` instead.

- [x] AD-289 Dynamic category page description — operator: the buying
      guide at the bottom of a category page (main *or* sub) was
      hardcoded; they wanted it editable per category, with an editor
      that supports backlinks, headings, pasted formatted content, and
      tables (reference: ryans.com's category pages).

      Extended the existing TipTap editor rather than adding Summernote
      (operator's call): `@tiptap/extension-table`'s `TableKit` with
      `resizable: false`, plus insert-table and row/column toolbar
      buttons that appear only while the caret is inside a table. Column
      resizing is off on purpose — it writes inline widths the sanitizer
      strips anyway.

      Storage is `Category.seoContentHtml` (migration
      `20260910215939_category_seo_content`, `PRISMA_CLIENT_EPOCH` → 26).
      `sanitize-html`'s allowlist gained `table/thead/tbody/tfoot/tr/th/td`
      and `colspan`/`rowspan` only — no `style` or `class`, so pasted
      markup cannot carry its own CSS into the page. Sanitized on save
      **and** again on read (`getCategorySeoHtml`): stored HTML is not
      trusted just because it was clean when written.

      Saving goes through the existing `saveAdminCategory` path
      (`CategoryInputFields.seoContentHtml` → `persistParsed`) so the
      description lands in the same update and the same audit entry as
      the rest of the category. `parseCategoryInput` stays client-safe —
      it passes the HTML through untouched, because the sanitizer pulls
      in Node-only code and the form imports that module.

      Two decisions worth keeping: (1) the edit page fetches the HTML
      itself instead of adding it to `ADMIN_CATEGORY_SELECT`, because
      that select feeds the whole category *list* and the copy can run to
      tens of kilobytes a row; (2) blank markup is stored as `NULL`
      (`isBlankMarkup`) — an emptied editor hands back `<p></p>`, not
      `""`, and storing that would pin the category to an empty panel
      instead of falling back to the built-in copy from
      `category-page-content.ts`. `<table>`/`<hr>` count as content, a
      lone `<br>` does not.

      `eslint` clean (`tsc` skipped — dev server live). Verified against
      real Postgres by driving Chrome over CDP as a real admin:
      **pasted** formatted HTML (heading + backlink + 3-row table +
      `onclick` + `<script>`) into the editor on `/admin/categories/laptops`,
      saved, and confirmed at `/category/laptops` — heading, styled
      table, links rewritten to `rel="noopener noreferrer"`, `script`
      count 0, no `onclick`, `window.__pwned` undefined, no table or body
      overflow at 1440px or 390px. Toolbar path checked separately:
      insert table → 3 rows / 3 `th`, Add row → 4, Add column → 4 cells.
      Sub-category `notebook` renders its own copy the same way, and
      clearing the editor restored the built-in "Shop Notebook at Techno
      House" block. All test content reset to `NULL` afterwards.

- [x] AD-290 Product card spacing pass — operator: "vitore aro kichu gap
      ache, title ta bold hobe, margin padding shundor kore shajao jate
      unprofessional na lage."

      Measured the card in the browser before touching it, and the dead
      air was not the padding. `aspect-square` + `object-contain` put a
      3:2 product photo in a 264px square box and letterboxed it by ~54px
      top and bottom — that empty band read as the gap. Media box is now
      `aspect-[4/3]` with `p-2.5`/`sm:p-3`, which brought letterboxing to
      0px on the seed photography, plus a `border-b border-border/60` so
      the photo zone reads as a deliberate band instead of floating.

      Title went `font-medium` → `font-bold` with `leading-[1.3]` and
      `tracking-tight`. Its two-line reservation (which aligns prices
      across a grid row) moved from `min-h-10` to `min-h-[2.6em]` so it
      tracks the line height exactly instead of leaving stray pixels
      under every one-line title.

      The warranty line now renders even when a product has no warranty
      label (`min-h-[1.0625rem]`, empty). Without it, cards missing that
      line were the shortest in their row and `mt-auto` pooled all the
      slack into one wide gap above their buttons — which is what made
      the grid look uneven. With both the title and this line at fixed
      heights every card in a row is identical and `mt-auto` has no slack
      left to pool. Rhythm tightened to one step throughout: `pt-3` body,
      `mt-2` price, `mt-1` warranty, `pt-3` buttons.

      `eslint` clean (`tsc` skipped — dev server live). Verified over CDP
      at 1440px and 390px: homepage card 435px → 368px tall with the same
      content, `distinctHeights` is a single value on `/`, `/category/
      laptops` and `/search` (13, 2 and 2 cards — no card is a different
      height from its neighbours any more), title `font-weight: 700`
      everywhere, 0 horizontal overflow at either width, and the hover
      action icons still sit inside the shorter media box (4px clear of
      its bottom edge, 10px of its right).

      `CatalogProductCard` in the same file has no callers anywhere in
      `app/`, `features/` or `components/` — left untouched, but it is
      dead code worth deleting on a future pass.

- [x] AD-291 Homepage "About" block made admin-editable + design-studio
      route 404 diagnosed — operator: the about section at the bottom of
      the homepage has to be dynamic because SEO copy goes there
      (references: startech.com.bd, dazzle.com.bd, ryans.com), and
      `/admin/design-studio/pages` was returning "Page not found".

      **Homepage block.** Stored as sanitized HTML on
      `SEOConfiguration.contentHtml` for the path "/" row (migration
      `20260911053226_seo_page_content`, `PRISMA_CLIENT_EPOCH` -> 27); the
      path-null row still carries only the <head> defaults, so meta and
      on-page copy stay separable. Edited from a "Homepage content" card
      on the existing `/admin/seo` screen behind `seo.manage` +
      same-origin, with an audit entry. `HomeStoreInfo` renders it in
      full when set and falls back to the built-in block when empty.

      Two things reading StarTech's actual markup changed, beyond the
      literal ask. Its block is h1 + 8 h2 + paragraphs carrying **62
      internal links** and no read-more toggle. So (1) the block renders
      expanded — collapsing SEO copy works against why it is there — and
      (2) `sanitizeBlogBody` no longer forces `target="_blank"` on every
      link. It used to rewrite all of them without looking at the href,
      which turns ordinary internal navigation into a new tab; now only
      off-site links get `_blank` + `noopener noreferrer`. That affects
      blog and CMS page bodies too. Headings start at h2 because the hero
      already owns the page h1 — StarTech puts an h1 here, we cannot.
      `.th-rich-text` prose is capped at 78ch (tables stay full width)
      and the first block's heading margin is zeroed against the panel
      padding.

      **Prisma pool leak (found the hard way).** Bumping
      `PRISMA_CLIENT_EPOCH` discards the cached client but nothing ever
      called `$disconnect()` on it, and `idleTimeoutMillis: 0` means the
      orphaned pool never releases a socket. A few epoch bumps in one
      `next dev` session walked Postgres to `max_connections` and every
      page 500'd with "remaining connection slots are reserved for
      non-replication superuser connections". `getPrisma()` now releases
      a client before discarding it. Already-leaked pools only die with
      the process, so this needed a dev-server restart to clear.

      **The 404 was not code.** Every *static* admin segment at depth 3+
      was 404ing — the whole Design Studio tab set, plus
      `/admin/blog/categories` and `/admin/settings/general` — while
      dynamic ones like `/admin/categories/laptops` returned 200. Adding
      a throwaway `routeprobe/page.tsx` under `design-studio/` forced
      Turbopack to re-scan and all 13 routes came back 200; deleting the
      probe kept them working. Stale dev route cache. If it recurs: stop
      dev, delete `.next/dev`, restart — no source change fixes it.

      The CMS pages at `/admin/design-studio/pages` already used
      `RichTextEditor`, so they picked up table support from AD-289 for
      free — verified the table tools render on the About editor.
      Creating brand-new pages is still unsupported (each needs its own
      storefront route); the list page says so.

      `eslint` clean on every touched file (`tsc` skipped — dev live).
      Verified over CDP: pasted copy with 2 h2 + h3 + table + 7 links ->
      6 internal same-tab, 1 external `_blank noopener noreferrer`,
      `<script>` and `onclick` stripped, exactly 1 h1 on the page, no
      overflow at 1440px or 390px.

- [x] AD-292 Homepage PC Builder section redesign — operator: make the
      section prettier, give it a professional UI.

      It was a remote Unsplash photo of a PC case beside three
      hairline-separated text blocks — generic, and it said nothing about
      what the tool does. Replaced the photo with a preview panel built
      from `BUILDER_SLOTS`, the same list the real builder renders: every
      slot as an icon chip using the existing `BuilderSlotIcon`, a
      "10 required slots" count derived from the data, and a
      compatibility footnote. It now shows the product instead of

      Deliberately no prices or part names in the preview. Any figure
      there would be invented, and a fabricated "running total" is
      exactly the thing this promo should not show.

      Right column got an eyebrow, a real `h3`, and the three features as
      icon rows in `primary-soft` tiles instead of bare text, plus an
      arrow CTA. Dark panel uses `bg-text` with two blurred token-colour
      washes (`secondary` warm top-right, `primary` teal bottom-left) for
      depth; `secondary` is the accent because that is already the PC
      Builder colour in the header CTA. No hard-coded hex anywhere.

      The eleven slots leave one trailing cell empty at both two and
      three columns, so the single optional slot (Monitor) spans two —
      the grid closes square at every width.

      The Unsplash `next.config.ts` remote pattern stays: mock catalog
      data in `lib/data/mocks/catalog.ts` still uses that host.

      `eslint` clean (`tsc` skipped — dev live). Measured over CDP at
      1440 / 1024 / 768 / 390: section 449px -> 532px tall on desktop,
      all 11 slot chips present at every width, CTA visible, and both
      body and section horizontal overflow 0 at each. Mobile checked

- [x] AD-293 "Shopping here" + "Guides" homepage sections redesigned —
      operator: give both a nicer, more professional UI.

      **Shopping here — found a real contrast bug, not just a style
      problem.** The icons and every "Learn more" link were
      `text-primary` (#125e6a) straight onto `bg-text` (#0e1a24), which
      measures **2.38:1** — under the 4.5:1 AA floor and barely legible
      on a real screen. Both now use `primary-soft`, the light tint of
      the same hue, at **15.21:1**. `primary` survives only as a tile
      fill behind a light glyph, where it is a surface rather than the
      thing being read (glyph on tile: 11.82:1; titles on ink: 17.62:1).

      Also gave the flat ink slab the same two blurred token washes the
      PC Builder panel uses, with the cells at `bg-text/60` so the wash
      reads through, and a `secondary` accent wipe that grows from the
      left on hover.

      **Guides.** Dropped the 01/02/03 numbers: they implied a sequence
      these three do not have — they are alternatives, not steps. Icon
      tile went to a `primary-soft` gradient with a ring that fills to
      solid `primary` on hover, the card now ends on its action above a
      hairline instead of trailing into empty space, and padding
      tightened (section 343px -> 328px). Same `secondary` hover wipe as
      the trust strip so both sections announce interactivity
      identically.

      No hard-coded hex in either file — every colour is a token.

      `eslint` clean (`tsc` skipped — dev live). Hover states verified
      properly via CDP `CSS.forcePseudoState` rather than synthetic mouse
      events, which do not trigger `:hover` in headless: wipe goes
      `scale: 0 1` -> `1` and the icon tiles go `primary-soft` ->
      `rgb(18,94,106)` in both sections. First probe of this reported
      "no change" because it read `transform` and `backgroundColor` —
      Tailwind v4 emits the `scale` property, and the Guides tile is a
      gradient in `backgroundImage`. Measured at 1440 / 768 / 390: all 4
      + 3 cards present at every width, 0 horizontal overflow on both
      sections and the body.

- [x] AD-294 Promo banner pair redesigned — operator: make that section's
      UI nicer and more professional.

      Third instance of the same contrast bug this session: the eyebrows
      ("TODAY'S DEAL" / "OFFER") were `text-primary` (#125e6a) on the ink
      scrim — 2.38:1, effectively invisible. They are now light
      translucent chips with a `secondary` dot, at full contrast.

      The scrim itself was the other problem: `from-text via-text/80`
      held the photograph under near-solid ink across most of its width,
      so the image read as a smear. From `sm` up it now goes opaque only
      under the copy and clears by the right edge. Below `sm` it stays
      heavy the whole way across on purpose — the title wraps to two
      lines there and runs most of the width, and the clearing gradient
      left white text on a bright photo at roughly 2.9:1. At `text/70`
      the worst case (pure white photo beneath) is about 6.4:1.

      CTA went from bare text to a translucent pill that fills solid
      white with ink text on hover — these tiles are entirely clickable
      but nothing in them looked like a control. Title up one step.

      **Two layout traps worth remembering.** The tiles were
      `aspect-[600/160]`, which collapsed them to ~94px between `sm` and
      `lg` — shorter than the new chip + title + pill stack. Adding
      `min-h-36` alongside the aspect ratio made it worse, not better:
      `aspect-ratio` then resolves *width from height* (144px x 600/200 =
      432px), so each tile grew wider than its 353px grid column and the
      whole page picked up a 64px horizontal scrollbar at 768. Fixed by
      dropping the aspect ratio for these tiles entirely and using
      `min-h-36 sm:min-h-40 lg:min-h-44` plus real vertical padding, so
      the tile grows when the title wraps. `min-w-0` on the grid item
      (against `min-width: auto`) was needed too but was not sufficient
      on its own.

      Isolated the overflow by hiding one section at a time and
      re-measuring `scrollWidth` — the blurred washes added to PC Builder
      and Shopping here also extend past the viewport but are clipped by
      their `overflow-hidden` parents and contribute nothing. Worth
      reusing that technique: a rect past the viewport is not proof of a
      scrollbar.

      `eslint` clean (`tsc` skipped — dev live). Verified content-fits-
      container at 1440/1280/1024/900/768/640/500/390 (no width clips its
      stack), 0 section and body overflow at 1440/768/390, and the CTA
      hover fill via `CSS.forcePseudoState` (`surface/10` -> solid white,
      text -> `rgb(14,26,36)`).

- [x] AD-295 Auth pages redesigned (customer + wholesale sign in and
      register) — operator: make these four pages a professional,
      good-looking, secure-feeling UI.

      All four run through `AccountAuthShell`, so the shell was the whole
      job. It was a bare form stranded on the left of an empty page with
      no card around it (the panel only got a background when an admin
      had set `authPanelColor`). It is now a split card: form on the left
      — first in the DOM, so it leads for keyboard/screen-reader users
      and stacks on top at mobile — and a dark assurance panel on the
      right.

      **Every security claim in that panel was checked against the code
      before it was written**, and the file carries a comment saying not
      to add one the implementation does not do: Argon2id with OWASP
      parameters (`lib/auth/password.ts`), the per-account/per-network
      throttle (`limitCustomerLogin` in `lib/auth/customer-auth.ts`), and
      httpOnly session cookies (`lib/auth/session-cookie.ts`). A security
      promise a sign-in page cannot keep is worse than no promise.

      Added optional `eyebrow` and `note` props so the wholesale routes
      read as their own thing: "WHOLESALE" label plus a callout saying
      prices stay hidden until an admin approves the account. The three
      Design Studio auth theme settings (`authBgColor`, `authPanelColor`,
      `authIllustrationSrc`) all still apply — the illustration moved
      inside the dark panel.

      **Behaviour change worth knowing about.** `SocialLoginButtons` used
      to render unconfigured providers as disabled buttons, deliberately
      ("honestly disabled rather than a link nothing backs"). On the
      redesigned page that put two dead grey controls above the form as
      the first thing a shopper saw, advertising an integration this
      store has not set up. Unusable providers are now omitted, which
      satisfies the same honesty argument. The divider moved into the
      component so the whole block disappears together instead of
      leaving an orphaned "or sign in with email" rule. Revert note is in
      the component's doc comment.

      `eslint` clean on all eight touched files (`tsc` skipped — dev
      live). **Functionally tested, not just looked at**: one real
      sign-in as the demo customer reached `/account` signed in (single
      attempt, so the 5-per-15-min email throttle was not burned), with
      `type="password"` and the `email` / `current-password` autocomplete
      attributes intact so password managers still work. Swept all four
      routes at 1440 / 768 / 390: 0 horizontal overflow everywhere, the
      panel present, and the form above the panel on mobile. The only
      sub-200px input on those pages is the collapsed header search,
      outside the auth card.

      Not done, offer standing: a show/hide toggle on password fields.
      It needs a shared `PasswordInput` across four separate form
      components, so it was left out rather than bolted on.

      Reused the pipeline rather than building a second inbox: the form

      Added `SUPPORT` to the `ComplaintSource` enum (migration

      28) rather than reusing `CONTACT_FORM`. `Complaint.source` is

      complaint — and `/contact` has no form yet, so reusing
      `CONTACT_FORM` would have muddled the two the moment it gets one.
      `createComplaintFromForm` takes the source as a third argument and
      still defaults to FOOTER, so the existing caller is untouched. The
      staff alert title switches to "New support request" for this source.

      No client JavaScript: a plain server action with redirect-based
      status. This is the page people reach when something is already
      broken, so the form should not itself depend on scripting.
      Signed-in customers get name and email prefilled.

      The aside carries an explicit warning never to send a password,
      full card number, or OTP — a public inbound form is exactly where
      people paste those.

      `listPublicPromotions`, `getAdminPromotion` and the admin campaign
      form (media picker + two inputs).

      validated on write (`normalizeLocalPath`) **and again on read**
      (`publicLocalPath`), because these columns feed straight into
      `next/image` and a `Link` href and are reachable by direct database
      edits or a future importer, not only by the guarded form.

      The rotated strip is a real text node using `writing-mode`, not an
      image of a word, so it stays readable to search engines and screen
      readers. It falls back to the campaign channel when unlabelled, and
      a campaign with no artwork falls back to its own name, summary and
      schedule rather than rendering an empty tile.

- [x] AD-299 Hero service/trust bar redesign — operator: "ekdom normal
      lagche" (it looks completely ordinary).

      It was a full-bleed white band on a white page, cells separated only
      by hairlines, with four identical pale-teal icon tiles. It read as a
      table row and dissolved into the background.

      Two changes did the work. First, it moved into the hero's own
      `max-w-catalog` rhythm as a single raised card (rounded, bordered,
      soft shadow) so it reads as a deliberate panel instead of a strip of
      page. Second, each item now carries its own token accent — EMI
      `info`, Support `primary`, online payment `success`, cash on
      delivery `secondary` — so the row reads as four distinct promises
      rather than one tile repeated four times. `warning` and `danger` are
      deliberately unused: an alarm colour on a reassurance strip says the
      wrong thing.

      Tailwind needs whole class names at build time, so the tones are
      written out in a `TONES` map rather than composed from a token name
      at runtime. Hover borrows the language already established by Guides
      and "Shopping here" — `secondary` accent wipe from the left, icon
      tile filling from tint to solid — so the whole page announces
      interactivity the same way.

      Contrast checked rather than assumed, since the accents are new:
      glyph on its own 15% tile measures 3.62–5.86:1 and white glyph on
      the solid hover fill 4.39–7.41:1, all clear of the 3:1 floor for
      graphical elements (`secondary` is the tightest at 3.62).

      `eslint` clean (`tsc` skipped — dev live). Verified over CDP: bar
      73px -> 86px at 1440 with the same content, 0 horizontal overflow at
      1440 / 768 / 390, clean stack to one column on mobile, and hover
      confirmed via `CSS.forcePseudoState` — wipe `scale: 0 1` -> `1` and
      the tile filling to `rgb(3,105,161)` (`info`) on the hovered card
      only, neighbours unaffected.

- [x] AD-300 Customer account review — operator: review the customer
      account, make it prettier and more professional, and add anything
      missing or not wired to the backend.

      **Three real gaps found, all fixed.**

      1. `/account/addresses` was an `AccountComingSoon` stub while the
         `Address` model had sat in the schema unused since it was drawn —
         nothing in the codebase read or wrote that table, and checkout
         collected the address as free text on every order. Built the real
         address book: list, create, edit, delete, set-default, with a
         20-address cap. Every query is scoped by the session's `userId`,
         never by an id from the request body, so passing someone else's
         address id resolves to "no longer exists". The "exactly one
         default" invariant is held in a `$transaction`, the first address
         saved becomes the default whether or not the box was ticked, and
         deleting the default promotes the next most recent.

      2. **No way for a signed-in customer to change their password.**
         `/account/forgot-password` only covers reset-by-email. Added
         `changeCustomerPassword`: re-verifies the current password even
         though the caller is authenticated (a session left open on a
         shared machine must not be enough to take the account over),
         then revokes every other session and issues this device a fresh
         one so the customer is not logged out of the tab they are in.

      3. Customer-facing copy leaked implementation detail — the Overview
         shortcuts read "Mock tickets", "Device-local list",
         "Same-type compare". Rewritten to describe the feature.

      **UI.** The identity line was small grey text under the heading;
      it is now a card with initials avatar, name, email and sign-out —
      the account area is where someone checks *which* account they are
      in. The ten-item nav got icons and an active bar, and below `lg` it
      scrolls horizontally instead of wrapping into four ragged rows.
      Profile split into two cards (details, password).

      `lib/account/address-limits.ts` holds the constants and row type so
      the client view never imports the Prisma-backed module — the
      client/server boundary bug class that has bitten this project three
      times already.

      `eslint` clean across `features/account/`, `lib/account/`,
      `lib/auth/customer-auth.ts` (`tsc` skipped — dev live).

      **Verified against the real database, not just rendered:** created
      two addresses, confirmed exactly one default, "Make default" moved
      it (checked in Postgres, not the DOM — the DOM read was stale), and
      server-side validation rejected an address with no city and
      surfaced "Enter the city." with nothing written. Password: wrong
      current password rejected, mismatch rejected, then a real change
      to a temp password and back again — both succeeded, this device
      stayed signed in, and active sessions went 3 -> 1 (the others are
      revoked and then pruned by `createCustomerSession`, which is why
      the revoked count reads 0). All test rows deleted; the seeded demo
      password is back to its original value.

      Caveat worth noting: saved addresses are not yet offered at
      checkout — checkout still collects the address as free text. Wiring
      the address book into the checkout form is the obvious next step
      and was not in scope here.

      was locked out the moment it was created. The demo customer only
      logs in because its phone is null. Turned OTP-on-login off in the
      admin screen; OTP-on-registration was left on. Re-enable it once a
      real SMS provider is configured, not before.

      * My first "set discount 12%" wrote 12 into the **Tier** field,
        because the label-matching heuristic grabbed the wrong input.
        The DB read caught it (`tier: "12"`, `discountPercent: 0`).
      * I then briefly believed the flat-percentage fallback was broken.
        It was not — those checks ran in a browser profile that had
        silently lost its session cookie between launches, so the page
        was rendering the signed-out prompt. Logging in and asserting in
        one browser session showed the fallback working. Do not trust a
        persisted CDP profile across `launch()` calls.

- [x] AD-302 B2B vs customer panel separation — operator: B2B credentials
      sign in through the customer form and open the customer panel; the
      header account icon does the same. A wholesale buyer should land in
      a wholesale panel, same structure but with B2B-only features.

      **Why it happened.** There is one session type. A wholesale buyer is
      the same `User` row with a `B2BAccount` attached, so the customer
      login form accepting those credentials is correct behaviour, not a
      bug — splitting authentication would mean two accounts for one
      person, two carts and two order histories. What was missing is that
      nothing downstream of the session knew the difference.

      Added `useB2BSession()` alongside `useCustomerSession()`, resolved
      once per request in the storefront layout so no client component
      hits the database. Everything else branches on it:
      * Header account icon -> `/b2b/profile` for a wholesale buyer,
        `/account` for retail, labelled with the company name.
      * The account nav gains "Wholesale account" and "My price list",
        but only when the account is **ACTIVE** — a pending application
        has no pricing to show.
      * The identity card carries a wholesale badge (active / pending /
        suspended), because pricing on every page depends on that state.
      * `/b2b/profile` now renders inside `AccountShell`, so wholesale
        and retail are one panel rather than two unrelated layouts.

      **New B2B-only feature: `/b2b/pricing`, "My price list".** Retail
      beside what this account actually pays, the saving, the order
      minimum, and a "Negotiated" tag on products with their own row. It
      resolves every line through `resolveB2BPricing` — the same function
      the product page and `create-order` use — so the list cannot quote
      a price checkout would refuse. `getMyB2BPriceList` returns null
      unless the account is ACTIVE.

      `eslint` clean (`tsc` skipped — dev live). Verified with both
      accounts in single browser sessions: B2B sees 12 nav entries
      including the two wholesale ones, the wholesale badge, and 25
      priced lines (Tk 42,800 -> Tk 37,664 at the 12% rate); retail sees
      exactly the 10 original entries, no badge, header to `/account`,
      and `/b2b/pricing` gated with **0 price rows leaked**.

      Process note: do not trust a persisted CDP profile between
      `launch()` calls — cookies are not always flushed, and two probes
      here reported "signed out" states that were artefacts, not bugs.
      Log in and assert inside one session.

- [x] AD-305 Order numbers are now a short numeric sequence — operator:
      "order id ta just number hobe but aro choto hobe, ekta smart
      sequence hobe.. just number only."

      Was `TH-20260906-DCAB44A9` (20 chars: prefix, date, random hex).
      Now `100001`, `100002`, … — six digits, nothing else.

      **Drawn from a Postgres sequence, not computed.** `MAX(number) + 1`
      is a race: two checkouts committing at the same instant read the
      same maximum and fight over one number. Order numbers go on
      invoices and into refund and support threads, so "usually unique"
      is not good enough. `nextval` never hands the same value to two
      callers and deliberately does not roll back — a failed checkout
      leaves a gap rather than freeing the number for reuse. Gaps are
      harmless; a number shared by two orders is not. The old generator
      needed a 3-attempt retry loop to dodge random collisions; that loop
      is now belt-and-braces rather than load-bearing.

      Starts at 100001 so numbers are six digits from the first order and
      do not advertise how many orders the store has taken. (Worth
      knowing: a strict sequence still lets someone who places two orders
      infer volume between them. A gapped or interleaved scheme would
      hide that, at the cost of the tidiness asked for here.)

      Migration `20260911213126_order_number_sequence` creates the
      sequence only — no data change. The 8 existing `TH-…` orders keep
      their numbers: they are printed on invoices and quoted in support
      threads, so renumbering history would break every reference.
      Nothing parses the number for meaning, lookups are plain string
      matches, and `looksLikeOrderNumber` already accepted bare 5–12
      digit input, so both formats coexist and public tracking works with
      either.

      `eslint` clean (`tsc` skipped — dev live). **Verified:** 20
      concurrent `nextval` draws returned 20 distinct values; three
      concurrent transactions each drawing and writing to `Order.number`
      produced 100001/100002/100003, all unique, all six digits, all read
      back from the table, then deleted and the sequence reset to 100001.

      **Not verified, and worth saying plainly:** I did not place an
      order through the checkout UI end to end. Customer login is rate
      limited right now by this session's own repeated test logins
      (`customer.login.email` 5/5, `customer.login.ip` 10/10), and the
      guest checkout path would not render its form. The sequence-to-
      column path is proven; the checkout form wiring around it is
      unchanged code and was not re-exercised.

- [x] AD-306 Palette rebased on the Techno House logo — operator supplied
      the logo (electric blue mark glowing over near-black) and asked for
      the site's colours to derive from those two, applied wherever each
      suits, "professional".

      Every storefront colour routes through the `@theme` tokens in
      `app/globals.css` — checked first, and no storefront file hard-codes
      a brand hex — so this was a single-file change. The 377 `#3897f0`
      literals are **all** in admin (97 files, 0 storefront); the admin is
      an internal tool and already blue, so it was left alone.

      * `primary` #125e6a teal -> **#0b5ed7**. Deliberately not the vivid
        glow blue from the logo (#0a6cff): that measures 4.56:1 on white,
        clearing AA by a hair with nothing left for hover or small text.
        The chosen shade is the same hue one step deeper — 5.84:1 both as
        link text on white and as white on a filled button.
      * `text` -> **#070b14**, the logo black with a slight blue cast so
        dark panels read as brand rather than grey. 19.68:1 on white.
      * Neutrals cooled toward blue: background #f4f7fb, surface-muted
        #eef2f9, border #d6deea, text-muted #59637a (6.02:1 on white).
      * `primary-soft` -> #e8f0fe, a true tint; primary on it is 5.1:1.

      **Two defects found while measuring, fixed in the same pass:**
      * `secondary` #b8612c was **4.39:1 on white — quietly failing AA**,
        and had been for the whole project. Darkened to #ad5a28 (4.92:1),
        still 4.0:1 on the dark panel.
      * `info` #0369a1 was nearly the new primary (RGB distance 56), so an
        info alert would have read as brand chrome. Moved to teal #0f766e
        — the most separable candidate at distance 108 — keeping 5.47:1.

      Orange kept as the accent on the operator's call: on a site this
      blue, the things meant to be clicked need a hue that is not blue.
      success/warning/danger untouched — they are signals, not branding.

      Re-checked the contrast work from earlier in this session against
      the new values rather than assuming it still held: AD-299 service
      bar glyphs are 4.03–4.67:1 on their tiles (floor 3), AD-293 trust
      strip text is 17.18:1 and its glyph tile 13.32:1. All still pass.

      Verified on the running site: resolved custom properties match the
      intended values exactly, and no `125e6a` or `b8612c` remains in the
      rendered HTML.

      Note for later: the storefront and the admin now run different
      blues (#0b5ed7 vs #3897f0). Harmonising the admin is a 97-file
      find-and-replace, worth doing as its own task.

- [x] AD-307 PC Builder button turned into a two-colour brand gradient —
      operator: "pc builder button er bg ta 2ta color er gradiant hobe,
      orange ta remove kore daw, baki shine effect thakbe,, logo er 2ta
      color."

      `.th-cta-sheen` in `app/globals.css` now carries **two stacked
      backgrounds**: the moving sweep highlight on top, the brand gradient
      (primary -> secondary) underneath. The trap here is that a CSS
      animation on `background-position` must list a position for *every*
      layer or the shorthand resets the ones it omits — `@keyframes
      th-cta-sweep` therefore names both positions at each step, which is
      why the gradient stays put while only the sheen travels.

- [x] AD-308 Brand colours applied to the Top Categories icons — operator
      supplied a screenshot of the green icon tiles: "ekhaneo same color
      gula."

      The tiles were on a green ramp left over from the pre-rebase
      palette. Moved onto `primary` / `secondary` tokens so they follow
      any future palette change rather than needing a second pass.

- [x] AD-309 Hero slider CTA made visible without hover — operator:
      "slider hove chara button ta visible na eta visible kore daw."

      **Root cause worth remembering: `cn()` in this project is a plain
      `join()`, not `tailwind-merge`.** The CTA used `variant="ghost"`
      (which sets `bg-transparent`) with `className="bg-surface"` passed
      in to override it. Both classes survive into the compiled CSS, and
      the cascade — not the argument order — picks the winner, so the
      button rendered transparent until `:hover` supplied a background.

      Fixed properly by adding a real `light` variant to
      `components/ui/button.tsx` rather than fighting the cascade:
      `bg-surface text-text shadow-sm hover:bg-surface-muted
      hover:shadow-md`. Any future "my className override does nothing"
      bug in this codebase is almost certainly this same cause; the real
      fix is to swap `cn()` for `tailwind-merge`, still open.

- [x] AD-310 Scrim, overlay copy and buttons removed from the hero slider
      and side images — operator: "slider and img er upor color overly
      lagbe na.. text and button o lagbe na ekhane direct real image ta
      jeno clear dekha jay."

      `home-hero-slider.tsx` / `home-hero.tsx`: the gradient scrim and all
      overlay text are gone and the whole slide is now a single `Link`.
      Two accessibility consequences had to be handled, since removing the
      visible text removes the link's accessible name and the overlay
      button was the only focusable target: each slide link carries an
      `aria-label`, and off-screen slides are `tabIndex={-1}` so keyboard
      focus cannot land on a slide nobody can see.

      operator: "jekhane discount thakbe … Save Extra Tk 350 on various
      offer emon kore likha thakbe, value different hobe products wise."

      `features/catalog/product-card.tsx` renders the discount as money
      saved beneath the price. The slot is **height-reserved**
      (`min-h-[1.05rem]`) and rendered even when a product has no
      discount, so a grid mixing discounted and full-price products keeps
      one row height — otherwise every row would step to the height of
      its tallest card. Verified on `/shop?sort=discount`: all cards in
      the grid measured a single distinct height.

- [x] AD-312 Hover removed from the home service bar; icons solid —
      operator: "ekhane over effect lagbe na. just icon gula solid color

      `features/home/home-service-bar.tsx` tones reduced to four flat

- [x] AD-313 Logo size control added to Design Studio — operator:
      "logo size thik korar jonno, ekhane ekta option thakbe jekhane
      pixel komano barano jabe."

      New `lib/business/logo-size.ts`: `LOGO_HEIGHT_MIN/MAX/DEFAULT`
      (16 / 96 / 32), `clampLogoHeight()`, and `logoStyle()`. **Height
      only** — width follows the artwork via `width: auto`, because
      offering both axes lets an admin squash their own logo, and the
      max-width cap scales with the height so a wide logo is never
      silently shrunk below the height that was asked for. The module
      imports nothing, so client components can read the bounds without
      dragging server code into the browser bundle (the recurring
      module-boundary bug class in this project).

      Clamped on read as well as on write: a row that predates the
      migration, or one edited directly in the database, still renders at
      a sane size. Migration `20260912184106_site_logo_height`.

      **Verification note:** my first CDP check reported the save working
      while the database still held 32. Synthetic `blur` events do not
      trigger React's `focusout` handler, and my "toast appeared" probe
      was matching the input's own value. Re-run with real dispatched
      mouse and key events, and confirmed against the database rather
      than the DOM.
